3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Galvos/Mirrors
Marking of characters is accomplished due to the movement of two mirrors, one for the X axis and one for Y axis formation of
characters.
Each mirror is glued to a galvanometer which moves the mirror based on signals generated on the Chameleon board and trans -
mitted via the CP- Light board.
The use of both X and Y axis mirrors means that it is not necessary for the product being marked to be moving as is necessary in
many other methods of marking. One mirror creates the horizontal portion of characters while the other is used for the vertical
portion of the characters.
There are four different marking heads, each is named for the size of the mirrors contained within. These include:
l SHC 60c or SHC60d or SHC60e (6 mm mirrors)
l SHC 100c or SHC 100d or SHC100e (10 mm mirrors)
l SHC 120c (12 mm mirrors)
l SHC 150c (15 mm mirrors) - not available on 3140
If it becomes necessary to replace one of the mirrors/galvos, then the entire galvo block (assembly with both galvos/mirrors
mounted) as well as the CP- Light board must be replaced. All marking head types require replacement as a set and all parts are
ordered as a single part number. Do not split up sets as they are tuned together before being sent as spare part.
Straight Out Marking Head Spare Parts
If the marking head is a SHC100/120/150 straight out configuration, then a fixed mirror attached to back of
galvo block must be removed from the old galvo block and attached to the new galvo block. This mirror directs
the beam to the galvos when the head is in straight out orientation. SHC60 heads have a mirror attached to
the rear of the marking head housing and not to the galvo block.
Type c, d and e Marking Heads
There are three different generations of galvos, which are differentiated by wiring differences in the connector
and marking head cable. Ensure that if replacing the galvos, CP- Light, or marking head cable that you use the
correct generation. For more information see "Identifying Marking Head Types" on page 165 .

Identifying Marking Head Types
There are three different generations of SHC60 and SHC100 marking heads: c, d and e types. When servicing the marking head,
CP- Light and marking head cable it is imperative that one is sure which revision is currently installed if it becomes necessary to
replace the galvo block, CP- Light board, or marking cable. Marking head Type D galvos/CP- Light2 are no longer available as spare
parts. It is not necessary to update to Type E galvos/CP- Light4 unless or until the existing galvos/CP- Light board fails. Upgrade
kits for Type E are available which include both the paired galvo block/CP- Light4 along with all 4 standard length marking head
cables for the Type E marking heads.
l SHC120c and SHC150c marking heads have only one generation.
l You can determine which revision of components you have by looking at the color combination of CP- Light Board Color
Laser serial number identification is not recommended because older lasers with Type C marking heads may have had com -
ponents replaced with Type D or Type E components. Physical identification based on color combination of CP- Light board and
marking head cable galvo connector is the most reliable method of identification.
Hardware identification is also recommended because the marking head type could be displayed incorrectly if the marking head
is not communicating and CMark is older than version 4.9.2. In older versions of CMark, the default marking head type displayed
is SHC60c when the CP- Light is not communicating, which could lead to incorrect part orders. CMark 4.9.2 and newer display No
Marking Head when there is a communications failure.

Rev AF

Marking Head
Importance of Focal Distance
Each marking head type has several possible lenses for use in various applications. When selecting a lens, consider the needs of
the application including the material that will be marked, the size of the marking, and any irregularity in the surface of the
product to be marked.
Figure 5- 12 below shows an example of two identical marking heads with different lenses: a 100 mm focal distance lens and a
254 mm focal distance lens. Focal distance refers to the optimal distance that the product must be from the lens for the perfect
focal point.
F IGURE 5- 12: Focal distance examples, illustrated.
When the laser beam leaves the output lens the beam itself is wide and the energy dispersed. As the beam nears the focal point,
the beam narrows and the energy becomes more concentrated, allowing for marking of the product. Each lens has a tolerance
wherein the product may be closer or further than the perfect focal distance and might still be marked. This area of tolerance is
called the Depth of Field . In general, shorter focal distance lenses tend to have less depth of field so slight changes in the dis -
tance from the lens to the product may cause the product not to be marked.
Shorter focal distance lenses tend to have a smaller, denser spot than do longer focal distance lenses. This means that, at the per -
fect focal distance, the spot size of a long focal distance lens will be wider and have the energy dispersed across that wider area,
while the shorter focal distance lens has a smaller spot size with the energy concentrated into a smaller area. Though a 30 Watt
laser will always have 30 Watt output, the focal distance of the lens determines the size and density (concentration of energy) of
the beam at the focal point.
The total area available for marking at one time is called the Mark Field . Longer focal distance lenses provide a larger marking
field allowing for either larger messages or, in the case of products being marked while moving, they allow a longer period in
which to mark the product.
173
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Following are descriptions of conditions using a shorter or longer focal distance lens create.
Shorter focal length lens:
l Product must be closer to head
l Less depth of focus (tolerance)
l Smaller, hotter spot
l Smaller marking field size
Longer focal length lens:
l Product must be further from head
l Larger depth of focus (tolerance)
l Wider, less dense spot (good for delicate substrates)
l Larger marking field
3640 Output Lens
The lenses designed for the 3640 have a broadband coating that prevents back reflection into the marking head. Though lenses
from previous models will fit in the 3640, they should not be used due to this lack of special coating. The exception to this are the
500 mm and 600 mm focal distance lenses which do not require this broadband coating.
Lenses with broadband coating are noted in the parts description with BBAR. These broadband coated lenses may be used in
31x0 and 33x0 models.
Working Distance
Lenses for the 3340/3140 /3640 are described by their focal distance, such as f=150 mm. This focal distance is the distance
between the lens itself and the focal point, which is where the product should be placed in order to be marked. To avoid damage
to the lens from personnel scratching the lens with rulers or other instruments, the Working Distance is a slightly different meas -
urement that is used to allow the user to measure from the bottom of the marking head (outside the lens holder).
Printing out of Focus
Some materials will print better out of focus. Always attempt marking first at working distances shown in
tables below, If desired print quality is not achieved, try increasing or decreasing the distance between the
marking head and the product slightly to see if quality improves. Only slight adjustments should be made or
code may disappear completely if too far away from suggested working distance.
When measuring the working distance, measure from the block of the head and not the lens.
174

Each lens has a focal distance and a working distance assigned to it based on how the optic is milled. If the focal distance is
known, then the working distance can be found in most user interfaces. The location of the information is based on the user inter -
face being used.

Best Practices 3640
In order to achieve the best marking quality and to avoid damage the following best practices should be observed:
Ensure the lens is clean
Due to the high output energy of the 3640, extra care should be taken to ensure that the output lens is clean. If fibers exist on
the lens, the beam may cause the fiber to melt/fuse to the lens. Other materials such as grease may cause the beam to be reflec -
ted back into the marking head, potentially causing damage to the internal parts.
177
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
A compressed air ring, which blows a clean stream of (customer supplied compressed) air across lens, is recommended. This com -
pressed air ring functions as a lens holder. There is no compressed air ring available for the SHC150c because the lens holder is
recessed into the marking head.
Following are the compressed air rings for the 3640:
l AL- SP72565 Compressed Air Supply SHC100/120 for focal distance lenses equal to 100/150 mm
l AL- SP71766 Compressed Air Supply SHC100/120 for focal distance lenses equal to or greater than 200 mm
l AL- SP72094 Compressed Air Supply SHC60 for all focal distance lenses
Avoid Perpendicular Angles
Wherever the beam hits a surface at a perpendicular angle, the potential for back reflection exists. This potential is higher when
marking on more reflective materials such as metalized paper, plastic, or foil. When possible, it is best to avoid marking the
product at a perpendicular angle. The marking head should always be situated at a perpendicular angle to product to avoid dis -
torting the marking field size and shape, but if possible the code should be slightly offset from center of the marking field as
shown in Figure 5- 14 below .
F IGURE 5- 14: Offsetting marks to avoid perpendicular marking with a 3640.
178
Rev AF

Marking Head
Beam Turning Unit (BTU)
F IGURE 5- 15: 30 Watt laser with BTU.
Beam Turning Units (BTU) are precisely machined, modular units containing mirrors inside internal elbow joints. BTUs may be
inserted between the laser head and marking head to redirect marking output to a location otherwise inaccessible to the full laser
head and marking head.
F IGURE 5- 16: Three BTUs connected; example of routing around an obstacle.
BTUs are directional. BTUs coming as spare parts will have a sticker attached bearing an arrow that indicates the direction of the
beam path. The end of the BTU indicated by the arrow and should point away from the laser tube and toward marking head.
Two typical BTU assemblies are the 200 mm and 300 mm. The 300 mm assembly adds a straight tube to extend the BTU fur -
ther away.
179
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
F IGURE 5- 17: 200 mm BTU.
F IGURE 5- 18: 300 mm BTU.
BTU Components
In addition to the internal mirrors, there are also several pieces that allow the BTU to stay in place while joined to the laser head,
marking head, or other BTUs/BUS (Beam Unit Straight).
Each BTU must be fitted on each side with a body clamp. This clamp attaches the BTU to the neighboring piece.
A centering bushing sits inside the body clamp and BTU and then a Teflon sealing disk is placed between the BTU and body
clamp to act as a washer.
180
Rev AF

Marking Head
The centering bushing fits between the BTU and any other piece to act as a stabilizer.

Customer Interface
(Internal Connectors)
Inputs and outputs for the 3340/3140 /3640 laser system are connected via the Customer Interface (CI) located on the
Chameleon board. Depending on the rear panel installed on the laser, these connections to CI may be done via Quick Connectors
or Cable Glands. Quick Connectors are pre- wired from the rear panel to the CI. Pass- through cable glands allow cables to run
through for making direct connections to the CI. In both cases, the CI is the same and it is only the method used to connect that
varies.
The CI is comprised of seven connectors.

Customer Interface (Internal Connectors)
Default Bridges
There are several inputs on the CI that are active lows and when the input is 0V or not connected, an error condition will occur.
Error conditions are avoided by placement of default bridges (jumpers) to hold active low outputs in a high state by connecting
to 12V/24V outputs. These default bridges may be removed if external devices will be connected to the active low inputs or may
be left in place after installation if the active low inputs will not be utilized.

Customer Interface - Maximum
Frequencies
The Chameleon board has limitations on the frequencies of inputs/outputs as shown in Table 6- 4 below .

Encoder 240 kHz

Table 6- 8: Chameleon Board Inputs
Name Description Polarity
Start Marking Starts the marking process, waits for a trigger signal, reacts to High active
rising edge. Start has no function if Stop Marking is set to low.
Stop Marking Stops the marking process. This signal has to be set to high if Low active
not used (bridge to 24V). If Stop is low then Start Marking has
no function.
The Start and Stop inputs can control the marking process with signals from a PLC or other external device. The Stop Marking out -
put, if not used, must be tied to 12V/24V as this is an active low input. If 12V/24V is not applied to Stop Marking then the con -
dition is always active and therefore constantly in Stop mode with Start disabled.
Table 6- 9: Chameleon Board Outputs
Name Description Polarity
Laser Ready Indicates that the system has completed the boot process and is ready High active
for operation. Entries via the digital inputs are accepted only if this
signal is set. Key switch must be turned and no errors occurred.
Marking Indicates that the system is actively marking. High active
Ready to Mark Indicates that all necessary data and a Start signal have been received High active
by the laser. The system is ready to mark and waits for a trigger signal.
The red emission LED on the supply unit and marking head are on.
Shutter Closed Indicates that the beam shutter is closed. High active
The outputs on X22 are often used to drive peripheral devices such as an alert light beacon, fume extractor, or for monitoring via
a PLC .
Default Bridges
Several default bridges are put in place during manufacturing to hold active low inputs in the high state to avoid errors. These
are:

l Pin 3 - 7: None to Stop Marking (since Stop Marking will have 12V/24V applied None will share the 12V/24V)
l Pin 7 - 9: Stop Marking to Input None (will have 12V/24V applied by another bridge)
l Pin 9 - 11: Input None to 24V
If Stop Marking input will be used, remove the bridge to pin 7 and connect 12V24V from customer equipment.

Connecting an Encoder & Product Detector
The 3340/3140 /3640 supports only quadrature encoders. When configuring the Encoder in SmartGraph software the pulses per
rotation must be doubled in Product Registration/Line Setup. Do not double the pulses for the encoder in TCS or Clarity Laser Con -
troller.
Product detect signals must be PNP (sourcing). Pin 4 provides supply voltage for product detect and ground is provided on pin 8.
193
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Encoder & Trigger Detection
F IGURE 6- 4: Encoder and product detect connection.
Figure 6- 4 above shows the typical connection for an encoder and product detector for the 3340/3140 /3640 . It is not necessary
to use the Encoder Index track unless needed in the specific application. The Encoder Index track may be used as a Trigger in
some applications, but in that case the Encoder Index track wire should be connected to pin 7 (Trigger) rather than pin 5
(Encoder Index). It is also necessary to use a resistor across pins 4- 8 so that the system senses a load between these pins.
The laser system inputs are able to identify automatically if an encoder or product detect are connected.
l If a product registration/line setup is selected that uses product movement with an encoder, the software checks to see if
there is a load between X25.2 and GND_ CI (e.g. X25.6). If no load is detected, an error will be generated (No Encoder
Attached). The same mechanism is implemented for the trigger.
l If a product registration/line setup is selected which uses a product detector (Rising/Falling Edge or External), the software
checks if there is a load between X25.4 and GND_ CI (e.g. X25.8). If no load is detected, an error will be generated (No
Product Detector Attached).
The minimum load to be identified by the system for encoder or product detect is 20 mA. If no load is connected, either a No
Encoder Connected  or No Product Detector Connected message will assert.
194
Rev AF

Customer Interface (Internal Connectors)
Trigger Detection with no Trigger Attached Errors
No Trigger or Encoder Attached Errors
If using a dry contact as a Trigger, such as a fire signal from a PLC, or sharing encoder between two lasers,
then it is necessary to use a resistor across pins 4- 8 (trigger) or 2- 6 (encoder) on X25 so that resistance/load is
sensed by the laser system. This is only necessary in CMark versions older than CMark version 3.4. In lasers with
CMark version 3.4 or newer it is possible to disable these errors in the laser's Web Interface and no resistor will
be needed.
See "Disable No Trigger/Encoder Attached Errors" on page 413 .
F IGURE 6- 5: A resistor employed to allow use of a dry contact as a trigger.
Use of a Dry Contact as a Trigger with no Trigger Detection (CMark 3.4 or newer)
Beginning with CMark version 3.4, you may disable the errors associated with no load on the Trigger supply pin by logging into
the Web Interface and toggling ON the box next to Disable Trigger | Encoder Detached Errors . After changing this setting,
reboot the laser.
No resistor will be needed, but it will be necessary to use pin X25.12 (24V supply interface) as the Supply pin for the Trigger
(24V).

A typical use case for signals from X27 would be to monitor Good shot and Bad shot. Following are descriptions of inputs and out -
puts on X27.
l Shutdown - 12V/24V pulse to this input will initiate shutdown of laser system. Same function as pressing Standby button
on outside of supply unit.
l PC Connected - Output is set as soon as a PC with client application is connected. Signal is High Active.
l Good - Indicates that the last marking has been completed without a warning or an error. The output is reset with the next
trigger signal. Signal is Low Active.
l Bad - Indicates that the last marking was not completed due to a warning or an error. The output is reset with the next trig -
ger signal. Signal is Low Active.
Pin 7 is bridged to X33.3 in order to enable the CPD and FASS safety systems. Do not remove this bridge.

Input Descriptions
l Error Status Customer - Allows for input of error from peripheral device. This input was known as External Error on ACC3-
based systems. Input is an Active Low, so if it is not used it must be tied to 12V/24V. This is bridged by default from man -
ufacturing to pin 7 to tie signal high.
l Error Confirm - 12V/24V input confirms, but does not delete or save errors that have occurred on the laser system. Using
Error Confirm remotely completes same action as pressing Confirm button in SmartGraph or other user interface.
Output Description
l Error - The system is stopped immediately if an error occurs during the marking process. The output is reset with the next
Start, but may be set again if the error was not corrected. Active low signal.
l System Ready - Output when system has booted with no errors and is ready to receive inputs. Any error will result in Sys -
tem Ready signal to be set to low. Signal is an active high.
l Ack_ Job_ Selection - A high level indicates that the job selection process is finished. Low level indicates that the job selec -
tion process was not completed. Typically used with External Template Selection. Signal is a high active.

X30 - External Template Selection
Signals for External Template Selection are received via X30. External Template Selection allows a remote device such as a PLC to
remotely change the current template. The external device sends a 24V binary pulse signal representing a number. That number
matches a template in an already compiled template list in SmartGraph. After receiving the 24V signal on the Strobe input, the
laser system changes to the template matching the number received.
Location
200

X30.6 IN JobSelect5
X30.7 IN JobSelect6
X30.8 IN JobSelect7
X30.9 IN JobSelectStrobe
X30.10 GND_ CI
X30.11 +24V_ CI
X30.12 GND_ CI
Up to 8 wires coming from the customer's PLC or other device are landed on the X30 connector representingup to 8 bits. The 24V
signal that actually executes the template change is connected on pin 9. All incoming signals must be held for at least 10 mil -
liseconds in order to be recognized.

X33 - Internal Connections
Most connections on X33 on the Customer Interface (CI) should be left with default bridges in place. The bridges in place on the
X33 connector allow for functionality of the safety system including CPD and FASS boards.
Location
F IGURE 6- 8: X33 location.
Description
Table 6- 14: X33 Pin Descriptions
Pin Direction Description
1 IN External Key Switch In
8 OUT External Key Switch Out
The only function that can be used on X33 is the Remote Key switch. By connecting remote switch to pins 1 and 8, the user could
activate laser power remotely. If this functionality is desired then either 1) the key switch inside supply cabinet must be bridged
or 2) the physical key left in the ON (armed) position all the time.
Default Bridges
Do not remove default bridges (jumpers) with the exception of External Key Switch. They are responsible for functionality of CPD
and FASS (where installed).

The new version of the laser alert light with 4 wires can only use input signals of the same polarity whereas the 5 wire version had

fore the shutter is open which occurs while in the marking mode. The amber light is illuminated when the laser is not Ready to
Mark. This state occurs when the Stop signal has been received and no errors are present. Both the red and amber lights will be lit
when an error occurs due to the low states of both the Error and Ready to Mark signals.

Safety System Overview 3140/3340
Safety System
Overview 3140/3340
3140 and 3340 laser models come equipped standard with an SPM board on current models while older versions may have a
CPD board as the standard. The SPM and CPD3 offer dual channel interlock and shutterlock capabilities, but do not offer an emer -
gency stop option.
An optional FASS board and CPD3 combination can be added for emergency stop functionality as well as a performance level E
rating. FASS must be used with CPD3 only. The SPM board does not have connectors necessary for FASS. If you need FASS func -
tionality but have SPM board in place, you must change the SPM to CPD3 and then add the FASS board.
You must configure all installed boards through the Laser Web Interface or TCS+ (CMark 4.7 or newer) to ensure correct func -
tionality.
Summary of differences in installation
l SPM: jumper from pins 1- 23 on X9 (Interlock open if no jumper)
l CPD3 alone: no jumper from pins 1- 23 on X9 (creates error if jumper)
l CPD3 + FASS: if wired as interlock, you must have reset switch on X9 pins 1- 23 (Interlock open condition until reset switches
closes and then opens again)
l Double pole, mechanical switches are recommended for use with all options.
l Single pole switches require special wiring to operate correctly and to avoid damage to the board.
l Mechanical switches should be used or else damage to contacts in the switches may occur. The SPM board has high cur -
rent used to hold relays open and is more likely to damage solid state/reed switches than CPD3.
F IGURE 7- 6: SPM board.
217
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
F IGURE 7- 7: CPD3 board.
F IGURE 7- 8: FASS board with cables from CPD3 connected.
Performance Level Ratings
SPM or CPD without optional FASS provides:
l Dual channel Interlock circuit
l Dual channel Shutterlock circuit
l No emergency stop available
l CPD3 - No performance level rating
l SPM only - Performance Level D can be achieved only through use of very specific parts and cables, including a manual
reset switch and specific door switch. See "SPM/SPM- 16A Wiring" on page 264 for details on achieving Performance Level D
with SPM only.
218
Rev AF

Safety System Overview 3140/3340
CPD with FASS provides:
l Dual channel redundant door circuit compliant to performance level D (function as Shutterlock)
l Door circuit extension
l Dual/redundant Emergency circuit complaint to performance E (function as Interlock with manual reset)
l Emergency circuit extension
l Manual emergency reset
l Customer must provide an emergency switch and an emergency release button in order to use the emergency circuit func -
tion
Expected Behavior: Interlock
Use in Mark on the Fly applications.
1. Shutter closes
2. System powers off supply to laser
3. Emission LED OFF
4. System reports “Interlock Open”
5. Without FASS
5 second delay between door close and laser power on again
6. With FASS
Requires reset signal before can continue
Expected Behavior: Shutterlock
Use in applications where door will be opened/closed frequently.
1. Shutter closes
2. Laser source remains powered
3. System reports "Shutterlock Open"
4. Emission LED ON
5. Resets when door is closed (no reset signal necessary)
SPM Board
The Safety Power Module (SPM) is the basic safety system board used in newer 3140/3340 lasers and all 7230/7330/7340/7440
laser models. SPM is a circuit board through which main AC power runs to both the electronics and laser power supplies. As long
219
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
as the laser is plugged in, the electronics power supply and Chameleon board are always supplied power by the SPM. The SPM
switches power for the laser power supply based on interlock circuit condition and software/logic signals from the Chameleon
board.
Performance Level Rating
The SPM has no Performance Level rating for the Shutterlock function or for Interlock. A Performance Level D can be achieved
only through use of very specific parts and cables, including a manual reset switch and specific door switch. See "SPM/SPM- 16A
Wiring" on page 264 for details. Use of any switches, relays, or cables other than those specified will result in no Performance
Level rating. If the specified switch and cable are used, Performance Level D is achieved. It is not possible to achieve a Per -
formance Level with SPM for Shutterlock no matter the components connected.
l If Performance Level D (Shutterlock/Door Circuit) or E (Interlock/Emergency Circuit) are required, a CPD3 and additional
FASS board should be ordered.
l The SPM cannot be connected to the FASS board.
l The SPM (white board) used in new 3140/3340 and all 7230/7330/340/7440 lasers is a different board from the SPM-
16A (red board) and SPM- 16A- FASS (blue board) used in 3640 lasers. Take care when ordering spare parts as these boards
are not interchangeable .
Location
The SPM board is mounted on the underside of the mounting plate within the supply unit. The mains power connector assembly
is part of the SPM board and is accessible through the rear of the supply unit. The SPM power connector assembly also contains a
fuse insert with two 8 Amp 250V fuses and a main power switch for the system. The SPM uses a power cord with C13 connector.
220
Rev AF

Safety System Overview 3140/3340
F IGURE 7- 9: SPM board mount location in 3140/3340.
Installing a New SPM - Bracketry Note
If a replacement SPM must be installed, it is necessary to remove the metal bracket surrounding the power
cord receptacle from the old board and to move it to the new board before installation. Spare boards do not
include this bracket. If the bracket is missing then one of the two screw holes needed to attach the SPM to the
rear of the supply cabinet will not be present.
F IGURE 7- 10: Power cord receptacle, fuse insert, and main power switch on SPM board.
221
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Compatibility
The SPM board (white) is only used in the 3340/3140 /3640 laser systems. This circuit board cannot be used with an optional
FASS board. If a Performance Level is required, then the SPM must be exchanged for a CPD and FASS boards. The CPD plus FASS
provides a dual channel emergency circuit with manual reset switch compliant to Performance Level E or door circuit (shutterlock)
compliant to Performance Level D.
Relays
The relays K1 and K2 are closed when the two circuits of the interlock wiring are closed. K3 and K4 are then driven by the
Chameleon board, which monitors the emissions LEDs on the marking head and keypad, as well as shutter status. The
Chameleon sends signals to close the K3 and K4 relays when shutter status is known and emissions LEDs are functioning. The K5
relay is part of the circuit that detects damage to the external wiring of the interlock system.
F IGURE 7- 11: SPM Relays.
Table 7- 7: SPM Relay Descriptions
Relay Driver/Function
K1 Interlock channel 1
K2 Interlock channel 2
K3 Chameleon board (software/logic/shutter)
K4 Chameleon board (software/logic/shutter)
K5 Part of error detection circuit for Performance Level D
222
Rev AF

Safety System Overview 3140/3340
Connectors
There are two ribbon cables mounted on the SPM that connect to the Chameleon board. These are the only connections used
with the SPM. There is no possibility to connect to an optional FASS board. If a FASS is required, use a CPD3 instead of an SPM.
LEDs and Fuse
F IGURE 7- 12: SPM fuse and LEDs.
Located next to the X9 connector (visible inside the supply unit between the Customer Interface section and the rear panel of the
supply unit) are five LEDs and a single fuse. The fuse is part of the error detection circuit that protects the SPM and associated
components in case of damage to external interlock wiring/cables. This fuse is replaceable with fuse specification Little Fuse 1
Amp Slo- Blo 125V max .
Table 7- 8: SPM LED Descriptions
LED Name Color Description
D5 Laser Enabled Green (On) Laser is enabled (both Interlock circuits closed)
D6 Shutter Enabled Yellow (On) Shutter is enabled (both Shutterlock circuits are closed)
D7 Shutter Closed Blue (On) Shutter is closed (“Shutter is Closed” = High)
D8 Shutter Command Blue (Off) Close shutter command (“Open Shutter” = Low)
D9 Laser Power Red (On) Laser power supply is on

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Cobra Power Distribution (CPD3)
The Cobra Power Distribution Board 3 (CPD3) is the basic safety system board for older 3340/3140 laser systems. Interlock
and/or shutterlock switches are wired into a connector on the CPD3 board. An application can use the CPD3 alone or in com -
bination with an optional FASS (FOBA ALLTEC SxxxS) board. To ensure correct functionality, you must configure the boards used
through the Laser Web Interface. See "Edit Laser Configuration" on page 408 . The 3640 laser system does not use the CPD3
board and instead uses either an SPM- 16A (red) or SPM- 16A- FASS (blue) with FASS when a performance level is needed.
Location
The CPD3 board is mounted on the underside of the mounting plate within the supply unit. The mains power con -
nector/fuses/power switch assembly are part of the CPD3 board and are accessible through the rear of the supply unit when the
power cord cover is removed. The two 250V 8 Amp fuses here are part of the main power circuit.
F IGURE 7- 13: CPD3 power switch/fuse holder/power cord assembly viewed from supply unit rear.

boards do not include this bracket. If the bracket is missing, then one of the two screw holes needed to attach
the CPD3 to the rear of the supply cabinet will not be present.

There are several versions of CPD boards used in Videojet lasers. The 3340/3140 uses a CPD3 board while other lasers may use a
CPD2 board. The CPD3 board may be used in place of CPD2, but CPD2 board cannot be used in place of CPD3. The CPD2 board
cannot drive the current necessary in lasers designed for use with CPD3.

FASS - FOBA Alltec
Sicherheitssteuerung (Safety
Controller)
The FOBA Alltec Sicherheitssteuerung (Safety Controller) (FASS), is an optional circuit board that when installed increases the per -
formance level of the safety system to Performance level d. The FASS is installed in addition to the CPD. The CPD is always in
place on all 3340/3140 laser systems.
The FASS board has two ribbon cable connectors X10 and X10a that are connected to the CPD board.
The following signals are monitored by FASS:
l Emergency switch 2 channels with contact extension and cross circuit detection
l Door circuit 2 channels with contact extension and cross circuit detection
l Shutter signals (status and control)
l Suction signals (status and control)
For instructions on connecting shutterlock, interlock, and the emergency switch, see "Safety System Installation - CPD and FASS"
on page 251 .
Location
The FASS board when installed sits on the underside of the mounting plate parallel to the 48V power supply.

The FASS is connected to the CPD board via two ribbon cables. When no FASS is installed these ribbon cables connect at both
ends to connectors on the CPD board . Therefore there is no need to order additional internal ribbon cables if FASS is to be
installed in the field.
F IGURE 7- 22: The FASS ribbon cables shown in yellow; the CPD only connector for ribbon cable shown in red.

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
SPM/SPM- 16A Wiring
There are three ways to wire the SPM (3140/3340/7230/7330/7340/7440 models) or SPM- 16A (3640 model) to interlock or
shutterlock system. The method used is determined by the desired function. If laser model has an SPM- 16- A- FASS or a CPD3
board, please refer to the sections specific to those boards.
All procedures contained here use a double pole switch. If using a single pole switch, see "Single Pole Wiring SPM/CPD" on
page 238 .
The following options can be achieved with the SPM/SPM- 16A board:
l Shutterlock (no Performance Level rating)
l Interlock with or without manual reset switch (no Performance Level rating)
l Emergency circuit with manual reset switch (compliant to Performance Level D)
Use Only Specified Parts
In order to avoid damage to components or to achieve stated Performance Level rating, it is important to use
the parts specified in the following wiring descriptions. Use of any parts other than those specified will result in
either no Performance Level rating or in damage to contacts of switches used for the interlock.
Shutterlock
For shutterlock functionality (mirrors go to shutter closed position, but laser is still powered), on connector X9 of the SPM/SPM-
16A remove the jumper on pins X9.5 to X9.16 and the jumper on X9.7 to X9.14. Connect double pole switch to pins 5 and 16
(shutterlock circuit 2) and pins 7 and 14 (shutterlock circuit 1). No reset switch is required for shutterlock function. Leave all other
jumpers in place.
When wired as a Shutterlock system, behavior is as follows when the door is opened:
1. Shutterlock Open status appears in SmartGraph or other interface
2. Mirrors go to the shutter closed position
3. Laser tube remains powered and capable of emitting radiation
4. Red emissions LEDs remain on
5. Must press Start to restart the marking process.
234
Rev AF

Safety System Overview 3140/3340
Interlock (No Performance Level)
To wire interlock with no Performance Level rating, remove jumpers 15- 19 and 17- 21 on connector X9 of SPM/SPM- 16A board.
Connect one circuit of double pole mechanical switch to pins 15 and 19 and other circuit to pins 17 and 21. Use of paired
screened cable is highly recommended. It is also recommended to create shield/ground connection on grounding bar (T shaped
cutouts in sheet metal between X9 and rear of supply unit cabinet). The grounding bar can also be used as strain relief for cables
by using cable ties to the T shapes.
Possible Equipment Damage - Mechanical Switches Only
The SPM/SPM- 16A Interlock circuit must be wired using mechanical switches or relays. Damage to contacts in
solid state/reed switches and relays will occur as the switches are directly driving relay coils on the SPM/SPM-
16A board. The peak current which is needed to switch the relays is too high for solid state/reed switches. The
Shutterlock circuit does not drive the relays on SPM/SPM- 16A, so solid state/reed switches may be used for
Shutterlock.
Using a manual reset switch is optional when no Performance Level is needed. To install a manual reset switch:
1. Remove the jumper from pins 1- 23 on X9 connector.
2. Connect a push button (mushroom button) style switch between pins 1- 23.
It is necessary to use a switch that pops back up on its own for the reset. If a toggle or other switch that stays closed
when depressed is used is in the closed position when laser is booted a fatal error will occur.
Behavior when wired for Interlock (with or without Performance Level):
1. Interlock Open status appears in SmartGraph or other interface
2. Mirrors go to shutter closed position
3. Laser tube powered down
4. Red emissions LEDs go off
5. If using manual reset switch, reset button must be pushed and released or Interlock Open status continues
6. Must press Start to restart the marking process
Emergency Circuit with Manual Reset Switch
In order to achieve Performance Level D, the following instructions and specified parts must be used or no Performance Level rat -
ing will be reached. Deviation of any sort will result in no Performance Level rating.
l You must use a Schmersal AZ- 16- 02ZVRK (minimum contact load 12 V / 5 mA) switch. You will also need to order an actu -
ator for this part based on the physical setup of safety enclosure. Videojet does not carry these parts.
l Use paired, screened (shielded) cable.
l Install a reset switch (push button or mushroom button; must pop back up after being depressed).
l You must use 24V_ fused output on X9 for Reset Switch to maintain Level D rating and to avoid errors causing an Interlock
Open status even when closed.
235
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training

Figure 7- 23 below shows wiring for both an interlock and a shutterlock. Typically only one or the other will be used. Which is used
depends on the desired behavior.
l If wiring for an interlock , leave Shutterlock jumpers (X9.5 to X9.16 and X9.7 to X9.14) in place.
l If wiring for a Shutterlock , leave Interlock jumpers (X9.15 to X9.19 and X9.17 to X9.21) in place.

leave the jumper from pin 1 to pin 23 in place. The system will not function correctly without either a jumper or a normally open
reset switch connected between pins 1- 23.

valent to achieve this Performance Level rating.
Though Shutterlock is shown in this drawing, you cannot achieve a Performance Level Rating using only SPM/SPM- 16A. You must

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Single Pole Wiring SPM/CPD
The safety system of Videojet lasers is designed to use a double pole switch. The use of a double pole switch builds in a redund -
ant circuit in case of failure of one side.
A single pole switch may be used to control the Shutterlock or Interlock functions, but because the system was designed to rely
on a redundant safety switching system, the wiring configurations detailed in the following sections must be used.
Damage to CPD
It is not sufficient to use a single pole switch on one of the Interlock or Shutterlock circuits and to leave the
default jumper for the other channel in place. On a CPD board, when only one channel is used (second channel
left jumpered) for Interlock, you will cause long- term damage to the relays on the CPD board.
Interlock/Shutterlock Open Won't Reset
On an SPM/SPM- 16A, when you only use one channel for Interlock and leave other channel jumpered the
Interlock Open will not reset unless you reboot the system. Use wiring detailed here for single pole switch to
avoid malfunction.
If a FASS board is present then a double pole switch should always be used and Interlock with FASS always requires a manual
reset switch to release the interlock. When FASS board is present and wiring as Interlock you must also supply a separate manual
reset switch as well.
Interlock Single Pole Switch Wiring
If using a single pole switch, then replace one Interlock jumper and install the switch in its place. Then, install jumpers from each
leg of the switch to its second channel as shown in Figure 7- 25 on the facing page .
l Note regarding CPD : If you only replace one jumper with a switch and leave the second jumper in place as is, the relay will
struggle to latch and a clicking sound will be heard while the Interlock is open. Leaving the wiring like this long- term will
cause damage to the board.
l Note regarding SPM or SPM16- A : If you only remove one jumper and replace with single pole switch once interlock is
opened you will not be able to reset without rebooting.
Use the Figure 7- 25 on the facing page and Figure 7- 26 on page 240 to wire Interlock function on CPD/SPM/SPM- 16A using
single pole switch.
238
Rev AF

Safety System Overview 3140/3340
F IGURE 7- 25: Single pole switch and jumper location for Interlock functionality.

Connecting to SPM/CPD3 X9 (Quick
Connectors)
If the Quick Connectors are on the rear panel of the supply unit, by default only six wires are connected to the Safety Quick Con -
nector from the X9 24- pin connector: four for the Interlock, one for Trigger Enable input, and one for 24V output. The Shutterlock
is jumpered on the X9 connector on the SPM/CPD3.
If no external customer safety control is attached to the quick connectors while in the default configuration, the Door/Interlock
Jumper Plug (PN AL- 74722) must be plugged into the quick connector.
242
Rev AF

Safety System Overview 3140/3340
To provide the 24V for Trigger Enable when the Interlock or the Shutterlock functions are governed by an external customer
safety control connected to the quick connector you must:
l Bridge 24V output to Trigger Enable input on the Customer Interface (X25.9 to X25.12)
- OR-
l Send 24V from customer safety control through wire 6 on cable AL- 75007 (shown in Table 7- 12 below ).
For SPM/CPD3 only (no FASS) Interlock with a double pole switch, connect as indicated in Table 7- 12 below . If using a single pole
switch, connect as illustrated in Figure 7- 26 on page 240 but do not connect to Emergency 1 In or Emergency 1 Out and cap the
Brown and Green wires on the 7 Pin DIN Connector and Cable (PN AL- 75007).
Not Recommended for Use with FASS Board
The safety connector on the Quick Connector plate is not recommended for use in systems with FASS boards
to connect Interlock/Emergency Circuit. This configuration requires a separate manual reset switch which can -
not be routed via the 7 pin DIN safety connector. Quick Connector can be used for FASS with reset switch, but
only if connected as Shutterlock/Door Switch and wiring inside supply unit is reassigned. See "Convert from
Interlock to Shutterlock" on the next page for wire assignment.

Trigger Enable
The active low input Trigger Enable and 24V output are also wired to the safety system rear panel quick connector. Trigger
Enable must be held high in order to acknowledge trigger inputs. This input is not always used by customer and with quick con -
nector panel Trigger Enable is bridged in the jumper plug to 24V output to hold Trigger Enable high all the time.
Wiring Trigger Enable
To use Trigger Enable, for example as a product present signal to the laser, use wire 5 (gray) on AL- 75007 cable/connector as the
24V output to switch/detect and wire 6 (pink) for Trigger Enable input (return from switch/detect).

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
If Trigger Enable will not be used then the monitoring of this function can be turned off in the Web Interface in Configuration
screen. See "Edit Laser Configuration" on page 408 for information on editing Configuration in Web Interface. You must be
logged in to the Web Interface to make changes to the Configuration screen. Additionally, the pink and gray wires coming from
AL- 75007 should be capped to prevent shorting if not being connected on customer side. It is also permissible to remove the pink
and gray wires from X25.9 and X25.12 and cap inside the supply unit instead. Ensure in all cases that if not using Trigger Enable
functionality that Laser Configuration is set to False with regards to Trigger Enable Voltage Needed or a jumper is placed from
pin 9 to pin 12 or X25 of customer interface.
Prevent shorting of 24V output
Remember to cap gray and pink wires coming from AL- 75007 if not connecting Trigger Enable and 24V on cus -
tomer side or shorting may occur.
Convert from Interlock to Shutterlock
The safety system quick connector is wired by default to behave as Interlock (no FASS present)/Emergency Circuit (FASS
present). This behavior includes turning off power to the laser tube and requires a 5 second delay after the Interlock/Emergency
Circuit is reset.
If the preferred behavior is for the tube to stay energized and when door is opened and mirrors to move to Shutter Closed pos -
ition, then it is necessary to reassign the wires inside the supply unit that connect the quick connector with the X 9 connector of
CPD3 or SPM board.
The following procedure explains how to change from Interlock to Shutterlock.
Personal Injury - Shock Hazard Warning
Lethal voltages are present within this equipment when it is connected to the mains electrical supply. Before
starting work, ensure that the laser system is completely detached from its power source. The Laser system is
energized as long as laser is plugged into power outlet.
1. Remove jumpers (two) on pins 7- 14 and pins 5- 16 on X9 connector of CPD3 or SPM board.
2. Move the following wires on X9 of CPD3 or SPM board coming from the safety system quick connector on rear panel of
supply unit based on Table 7- 13 below .
Table 7- 13: Wires to move, X9
Wire Color Signal Move from Move to
Green Channel 1 out X9.21 X9.7
Brown Channel 1 return (in) X9.17 X9.14
White Channel 2 out X9.19 X9.5
Yellow Channel 2 return (in) X9.15 X9.16
3. Insert one of the jumpers removed in step 1 into X9.15 - X9.19 and the other jumper into X9.17 - X9.21.
244
Rev AF

Safety System Overview 3140/3340
4. Connect the cable on the customer side to the door switch using the wire colors shown in the rightmost column of Table 7-

Single Pole Switch with Quick Connect
The quick connector for the safety system is intended for use with a double pole door switch. If a single pole switch will be used
then it is necessary to reassign wires inside supply cabinet on X9 connector of CPD/SPM board using the following instructions.
Interlock with Single Pole and Quick Connect
1. Disconnect White and Yellow wires from X9 on CPD/SPM. Cut or cap these wires to prevent shorting.
2. Place jumper from pin 15 to pin 17 on X9. There will now be both the jumper and Brown wire on pin 17 .
3. Place jumper from pin 19 to pin 21 on X9. There will now be both the jumper and Green wire on pin 21 .
4. Connect customer single pole switch to Green (out) and Brown (in) wires of AL- 75007 cable.
5. Cut or cap White and Yellow wires on AL- 75007 cable.
245
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
6. If Trigger Enable will be used, connect Trigger Enable to the customer side using the Pink and Gray wires.
- OR-
If Trigger Enable will not be used:
6.1. Remove the Pink and Gray wires at X25 pins 9 and 12.
6.2. Place a jumper between X25 pins 9 and 12.
6.3. Cut or cap the Pink and Gray wires on both the inside of the supply unit and on the cable.
Shutterlock with Single Pole and Quick Connect
1. Remove and cut or cap White and Yellow wires on both the inside and outside of the quick connector as well as the cable to
customer.
2. Remove two jumpers on X9: remove the jumper between pin 5 and pin 16 and remove the jumper between pin 7 and pin
14. Retain these jumpers for use in following steps.
3. Inside the supply unit, on X9 move the Green wire coming from safety connector over from X9 pin 21 to X9 pin 7.
4. Move the Brown wire from X9 pin 17 to X9 pin 14.
5. Place a jumper from X9 pin 7 to X9 pin 5. You should now have a jumper and Green wire in pin 7.
6. Place a jumper from X9 pin 14 to X9 pin 16. You should now have both a jumper and Brown wire in pin 14.
7. Place a jumper on X9 between pins 15 and 19.
8. Place a jumper on X9 between pins 17 and 21.
9. Connect the Brown (in) and Green (out) wires on the AL- 75007 cable to the customer single pole door switch.
10. Connect Trigger Enable and 24 V via AL- 75007 at the customer side.
- OR-
Remove the pink and gray wires from the X25 connector on the Customer Interface of the Chameleon board and
instead place a jumper between pins 9 and 12 on X25.
See "Trigger Enable" on page 243 for more information.

Safety System
Overview 3640
The 3640 laser comes standard with an SPM- 16A (Safety Power Module 16 Amp) circuit board that is the center of the safety sys -
tem in the laser. If a Performance Level of D (shutterlock) or E (interlock) is required then the laser should be ordered with the
optional SPM- 16A- FASS and FASS board. It is possible to achieve a Performance Level of D for interlock with the standard SPM-
16A board, but only when specific switch and wiring is used. Any deviation from the specified part will result in no Performance
Level rating.
SPM- 16- A provides:
l Dual channel Shutterlock circuit (no Performance Level rating)
l Dual/redundant Emergency circuit compliant to performance level D (function as Interlock with manual reset. Performance
Level D only when using specified parts and wiring)
l Dual channel Interlock circuit with or without manual reset switch (no Performance Level rating; must use mechanical
switches/relays not solid state)
l Customer must supply reset switch
SPM- 16- FASS together with FASS board provides:
l Dual channel redundant door circuit compliant to performance level D (function as Shutterlock)
l Door circuit extension
l Dual/redundant Emergency circuit compliant to performance level E (function as Interlock with manual reset)
l Emergency circuit extension
l Manual emergency reset
l Customer must supply reset switch
Both the SPM- 16A and SPM- 16- A- FASS are designed to be used with double- pole switches as two circuits are monitored.
The boards are distinguished easily by their color. SPM- 16A board is red and SPM- 16A- FASS is blue in color. You can see the SPM
board and its X9 connector through cutout in mounting plate between Customer Interface section of Chameleon board and rear
panel of supply unit.
257
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Expected Behavior: Interlock
Use an interlock in Mark on the Fly applications.
1. Shutter closes
2. System powers off supply to laser
3. Emission LED “OFF”
4. System reports “Interlock Open”
5. Close door/contact
- 5 second delay between door close and laser power on again
With manual reset switch
- Requires reset signal before can continue
6. System reports "Laser Ready"
Shutterlock
Use shutterlock in applications where door will be opened/closed frequently.
1. Shutter closes
2. Laser source remains powered
3. System reports ‘Shutterlock Open’
4. Emission LED ‘ON’
5. Resets when door is closed (no reset signal necessary)
258
Rev AF

Safety System Overview 3640
Safety Power Module 16- A (SPM- 16A)
The Safety Power Module 16 Amp (SPM- 16- A) is the basic safety system for the 3640 laser model. SPM- 16A is a circuit board
through which main AC power runs to both the electronics and laser power supplies. As long as the 3640 laser is plugged in, the
electronics power supply and Chameleon board are always supplied power by the SPM- 16A. The SPM- 16A switches power for the
laser power supply based on interlock circuit condition and software/logic signals from the Chameleon board.
Possible Equipment Damage - Mechanical Switches Only
The SPM/SPM- 16A Interlock circuit must be wired using mechanical switches or relays. Damage to contacts in
solid state/reed switches and relays will occur as the switches are directly driving relay coils on the SPM/SPM-
16A board. The peak current which is needed to switch the relays is too high for solid state/reed switches. The
Shutterlock circuit does not drive the relays on SPM/SPM- 16A, so solid state/reed switches may be used for
Shutterlock.
Performance Level Rating
The SPM- 16A has no Performance Level rating for Shutterlock function or for Interlock unless Interlock uses switch, cables, and
reset switch specified in "SPM/SPM- 16A Wiring" on page 264 . Use of any switches, relays, or cables other than those specified will
result in no Performance Level rating. If switch and cable specified are used Performance Level D is achieved.
l If Performance Level D (Shutterlock/Door Circuit) or E (Interlock/Emergency Circuit) are required an SPM- 16A- FASS and
additional FASS board should be ordered.
l The SPM- 16A and SPM- 16A- FASS are different boards.
l The SPM- 16A cannot be connected to the FASS board. The boards are easily distinguishable by color.
Location
The SPM- 16- A board is mounted on the underside of the mounting plate within the supply unit. The mains power connector
assembly is part of the SPM- 16A board and is accessible through the rear of the supply unit if power cord cover are removed.
F IGURE 8- 1: SPM- 16A power cord receptacle viewed from rear of supply unit.
259
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training

Installing new SPM- 16A
If a replacement SPM- 16A must be installed, it is necessary to remove the metal bracket surrounding the
power cord receptacle from the old board and to move it to the new board before installation. Spare SPM- 16A
boards do not include this bracket. If the bracket is missing then one of the two screw holes needed to attach
the SPM- 16A to the rear of the supply cabinet will not be present.
.
F IGURE 8- 4: Metal bracket that must be taken from old SPM- 16A and moved to new when replacing SPM- 16A.
Compatibility
The SPM- 16A is only used in the 3640 laser system. This circuit board cannot be used with optional FASS board. If functionality
of FASS is required, dual channel emergency circuit with manual reset switch compliant to Performance Level E or door circuit
(shutterlock) compliant to Performance Level D, then SPM- 16A- FASS is necessary for connection instead of SPM- 16A.

Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Relays
Relay Driver/Function
K1 Interlock channel 1
K2 Interlock channel 2
K3 Chameleon board (software/logic/shutter)
K4 Chameleon board (software/logic/shutter)
K5 Part of error detection circuit for Performance Level D
The relays K1 and K2 are closed when the two circuits of the interlock wiring are closed. K3 and K4 are then driven by the
Chameleon board which monitors the emissions LEDs on marking head and keypad, as well as shutter status, and sends signals
to close these relays when shutter status is known and emissions LEDs are functioning. The K5 relay is part of the circuit that
detects damage to the external wiring of the interlock system.

with the SPM- 16A. There is no possibility to connect to optional FASS board. If FASS is required please use SPM- 16A- FASS.

LEDs and Fuse
Located next to X9 connector which is visible from interior of supply unit between Customer Interface section and rear panel of
supply unit, are five LEDs and a single fuse. The fuse is part of the error detection circuit which is used to protect the SPM- 16A
and associated components in case of damage to external interlock wiring/cables. This fuse is replaceable and is part of the fuse
kit for the 3640.
LED Name Color When on
D5 Laser Disabled Green Laser is disabled (both interlock circuits are open)
D6 Shutter Enabled Yellow Shutter can open (both shutterlock circuits are closed)
D7 Shutter Closed Blue Shutter is closed
D8 Shutter Command Blue Open shutter command
D9 Laser Power Red Laser power supply is on
SPM/SPM- 16A Wiring
There are three ways to wire the SPM (3140/3340/7230/7330/7340/7440 models) or SPM- 16A (3640 model) to interlock or
shutterlock system. The method used is determined by the desired function. If laser model has an SPM- 16- A- FASS or a CPD3
board, please refer to the sections specific to those boards.
All procedures contained here use a double pole switch. If using a single pole switch, see "Single Pole Wiring SPM/CPD" on
page 238 .
The following options can be achieved with the SPM/SPM- 16A board:
l Shutterlock (no Performance Level rating)
l Interlock with or without manual reset switch (no Performance Level rating)
l Emergency circuit with manual reset switch (compliant to Performance Level D)
264
Rev AF

Safety System Overview 3640
Use Only Specified Parts
In order to avoid damage to components or to achieve stated Performance Level rating, it is important to use
the parts specified in the following wiring descriptions. Use of any parts other than those specified will result in
either no Performance Level rating or in damage to contacts of switches used for the interlock.
Shutterlock
For shutterlock functionality (mirrors go to shutter closed position, but laser is still powered), on connector X9 of the SPM/SPM-
16A remove the jumper on pins X9.5 to X9.16 and the jumper on X9.7 to X9.14. Connect double pole switch to pins 5 and 16
(shutterlock circuit 2) and pins 7 and 14 (shutterlock circuit 1). No reset switch is required for shutterlock function. Leave all other
jumpers in place.
When wired as a Shutterlock system, behavior is as follows when the door is opened:
1. Shutterlock Open status appears in SmartGraph or other interface
2. Mirrors go to the shutter closed position
3. Laser tube remains powered and capable of emitting radiation
4. Red emissions LEDs remain on
5. Must press Start to restart the marking process.
Interlock (No Performance Level)
To wire interlock with no Performance Level rating, remove jumpers 15- 19 and 17- 21 on connector X9 of SPM/SPM- 16A board.
Connect one circuit of double pole mechanical switch to pins 15 and 19 and other circuit to pins 17 and 21. Use of paired
screened cable is highly recommended. It is also recommended to create shield/ground connection on grounding bar (T shaped
cutouts in sheet metal between X9 and rear of supply unit cabinet). The grounding bar can also be used as strain relief for cables
by using cable ties to the T shapes.
Possible Equipment Damage - Mechanical Switches Only
The SPM/SPM- 16A Interlock circuit must be wired using mechanical switches or relays. Damage to contacts in
solid state/reed switches and relays will occur as the switches are directly driving relay coils on the SPM/SPM-
16A board. The peak current which is needed to switch the relays is too high for solid state/reed switches. The
Shutterlock circuit does not drive the relays on SPM/SPM- 16A, so solid state/reed switches may be used for
Shutterlock.
Using a manual reset switch is optional when no Performance Level is needed. To install a manual reset switch:
1. Remove the jumper from pins 1- 23 on X9 connector.
2. Connect a push button (mushroom button) style switch between pins 1- 23.
It is necessary to use a switch that pops back up on its own for the reset. If a toggle or other switch that stays closed
when depressed is used is in the closed position when laser is booted a fatal error will occur.
265
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Behavior when wired for Interlock (with or without Performance Level):
1. Interlock Open status appears in SmartGraph or other interface
2. Mirrors go to shutter closed position
3. Laser tube powered down
4. Red emissions LEDs go off
5. If using manual reset switch, reset button must be pushed and released or Interlock Open status continues
6. Must press Start to restart the marking process
Emergency Circuit with Manual Reset Switch
In order to achieve Performance Level D, the following instructions and specified parts must be used or no Performance Level rat -
ing will be reached. Deviation of any sort will result in no Performance Level rating.
l You must use a Schmersal AZ- 16- 02ZVRK (minimum contact load 12 V / 5 mA) switch. You will also need to order an actu -
ator for this part based on the physical setup of safety enclosure. Videojet does not carry these parts.
l Use paired, screened (shielded) cable.
l Install a reset switch (push button or mushroom button; must pop back up after being depressed).
l You must use 24V_ fused output on X9 for Reset Switch to maintain Level D rating and to avoid errors causing an Interlock
Open status even when closed.
Wiring Diagram
Figure 8- 5 on the facing page shows wiring for both an interlock and a shutterlock. Typically only one or the other will be used.
Which is used depends on the desired behavior.
l If wiring for an interlock , leave Shutterlock jumpers (X9.5 to X9.16 and X9.7 to X9.14) in place.
l If wiring for a Shutterlock , leave Interlock jumpers (X9.15 to X9.19 and X9.17 to X9.21) in place.
Figure 8- 5 on the facing page shows a reset switch for the Interlock connected from pin 1 to pin 23. If no Performance Level is
required, leave the jumper from pin 1 to pin 23 in place. The system will not function correctly without either a jumper or a nor -
mally open reset switch connected between pins 1- 23.

Safety System Overview 3640
F IGURE 8- 5: Diagram: Wiring for an interlock or shutterlock with reset switch shown.
Figure 8- 5 above suggests the wiring and parts needed to achieve a Performance Level D for Interlock (Emergency Circuit). You
must use paired, screened cables, a reset switch, and a specific switch (Schmersal AZ 16- 02ZVRK) or equivalent to achieve this Per -
formance Level rating.
Though Shutterlock is shown in this drawing, you cannot achieve a Performance Level Rating using only SPM/SPM- 16A. You must
use CPD+FASS for most Chameleon lasers or an SPM- 16A- FASS and a FASS board in 3640 lasers to achieve a Performance Level
Rating with Shutterlock.
It is recommended that incoming wires (if shielded) are stripped just inside the supply unit and tied down using cable ties to the
grounding bar near the rear of the supply unit. This creates a shield ground on these cables.

Safety Power Module 16 Amp - FASS
Version (SPM- 16A- FASS)
The Safety Power Module 16 Amp- Foba Altec Sicherheitsteurung (SPM- 16A- FASS) must be used in combination with a FASS
board. This combination is not standard on 3640 units and must be ordered as an option. The SPM- 16A- FASS board is not the
same board as the SPM- 16A.
SPM- 16A- FASS is a circuit board through which main AC power runs to both the electronics and laser power supplies. As long as
the 3640 laser is plugged in the electronics power supply and Chameleon board are always supplied power by the SPM- 16A-
FASS. The SPM- 16A switches power for the laser power supply based on interlock circuit condition and software/logic signals from
the Chameleon board.
If the SPM- 16A- FASS is installed then the optional FASS board must also be installed or system will not operate.
Performance Level Ratings
l Performance Level "d" for Door Circuit (Shutterlock)
l Performance Level "e" for Emergency Circuit (Interlock)
Location
The SPM- 16A- FASS board is mounted on the underside of the mounting plate within the supply unit. The FASS board is mounted
next to the electronics power supply. The mains power connector assembly is part of the SPM- 16A- FASS board and is accessible
through the rear of the supply unit if power cord cover are removed.

Safety System Overview 3640
Compatibility
The SPM- 16A- FASS is only used in the 3640 laser system and is not compatible with other laser models. This circuit board must
be used with FASS board or system will not be functional (laser power supply will not be enabled).
Relays
Relay Driver/Function
K1 Interlock channel 1
K2 Interlock channel 2
K3 Chameleon board (software/logic/shutter)
K4 Chameleon board (software/logic/shutter)
The relays K1 and K2 are closed when the two circuits of the interlock wiring are closed. K3 and K4 are then driven by the
Chameleon board which monitors the emissions LEDS on the marking head and keypad, as well as the shutter status, and sends
signals to close these relays when shutter status is known and emissions LEDs are functioning.
Relays K1 and K2 must close before K3 and K4 will be energized. The high inrush current is switched by K3 and K4.

SPM- 16A- FASS Wiring
The SPM- 16A- FASS can be wired in either of two ways:
l Door Circuit compliant to Performance Level "d" - functions as Shutterlock
l Emergency Circuit compliant to Performance Level "e" - functions as Interlock
Door Circuit
For Door Circuit functionality (mirrors go to shutter closed position, but laser is still powered), remove jumpers on connector X9 of
SPM- 16A- FASS from pins X9.5 to X9.16 and X9.7 to X9.14. Connect double pole switch to pins X9.5 to X9.16 (shutterlock circuit 2)
and pins X9.7 to X9.14 (shutterlock circuit 1). No reset switch is required for Door Circuit function. Leave all other jumpers in
place.
When wired as Door Circuit system behavior is as follows when door opened:
l Shutterlock Open status appears in SmartGraph Laser Operation tab or other interface
l Mirrors go to shutter closed position
l Laser tube remains powered and capable of emitting radiation
l Red emissions LEDs remain on
l Must press Start to restart the marking process
Emergency Circuit
A manual reset switch must be used if wired for Emergency Circuit. To install a manual reset switch:
1. Remove the jumper on pins X9.1 and X9.23 on the X9 connector.
2. Connect a push button (mushroom button style) switch between pins X9.1 and X9.23. It is necessary to use a switch that
pops back up on its own for the reset and not a toggle or other switch that stays closed when depressed. If the connected
switch is closed during the boot process, the laser will assert a fatal error.
3. Remove the jumper from X9.15 to X9.19 and the jumper form X9.17 to X9.21 and install a double pole switch in their
place.
Behavior when wired for Emergency Circuit (Interlock):
1. Interlock Open status appears in SmartGraph Laser Operation tab or other interface
2. Mirrors go to shutter closed position
3. Laser tube powered down
4. Red emissions LEDs go off
273
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
5. Reset button must be pushed and released or Interlock Open status continues
6. Must press Start to restart the marking process
Wiring Diagram
If using the Emergency Circuit, the system must be reset with a pulse from the reset switch after the door/contacts are closed.
Unlike the SPM- 16A board, the SPM- 16A- FASS can be connected using solid state or mechanical switches.

Installation
The laser system accessories can be connected to the system both externally and internally. Externally, accessories connect via a
quick connector plate. Internally, accessories are connected via a cable passage plate or via the standard hybrid connector plate.
When using the hybrid connector plate, some accessories are connected at the Chameleon Customer Interface while others are
connected on external Quick Connectors.
Detailed descriptions of available inputs and outputs are found in the Customer Interface (Internal Connectors) section. The Cus -
tomer Interface (External) section contains information on how to connect, but not detailed descriptions on all inputs and out -
puts available in the system.
Installation Pre- Checks
Shock Hazard Warning
Use test equipment safely and properly.
1. Check the incoming voltage from the mains power receptacle with a multimeter.
2. Ensure placement of laser will allow for proper airflow and cable allowances as shown in the Operating Instructions.
3. Check the laser shipping carton for damage. Check the shock/tilt watches on packaging.
4. Unpack the laser and check the condition of laser system.
5. Verify that the laser system in the box is correctly configured as ordered (model, IP, wavelength, marking head, lens).
6. Check that all ordered accessories are included.
7. Connect the laser system to a temporary power supply.
8. Position a target at the correct focal distance from marking head.
9. Create a Test Pattern template in SmartGraph.
10. Take sample mark on the target using the Test Pattern template. Does the laser mark the product as expected?
If the laser does not create a mark with no peripheral devices (encoder, product detector, etc.), then do not proceed with
installation until the issue is resolved.
11. Mount the laser. The mounting hole pattern can be found in the Operating Instructions for each laser model.
12. Set the customer interface voltage source on X19 and X20. The default configuration is 24V Internal source. See "X19 &
X20 - Supply Voltage" on page 95 .
13. Connect the accessories. See "Customer Interface (Internal Connectors)" on page 183 for more on connecting when
using cable gland plate and "Customer Interface (External)" on page 207 when using quick connector plate.
14. Connect the safety system.
For newer 3140/3340 lasers with an SPM board only and a safety connection with double pole switch, see "SPM/SPM-
16A Wiring" on page 264 . If using a single pole switch, see "Single Pole Wiring SPM/CPD" on page 238 .
277
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
For older 3140/3340 with a CPD only and a safety connection with double pole switch, see "Safety System Installation -
CPD3 Only" on page 247 for wiring CPD3 only safety. See "Safety System Installation - CPD and FASS" on page 251 for
wiring CPD3+FASS safety systems. If using a single pole switch, see "Single Pole Wiring SPM/CPD" on page 238 as well.
For 3640 safety connection with double pole switch, see "SPM/SPM- 16A Wiring" on page 264 and "SPM- 16A- FASS Wir -
ing" on page 273 . If using single pole switch see "Single Pole Wiring SPM/CPD" on page 238 as well.
15. Configure the Product Registration/Line Setup. Use the Kompensation template for best results when using an encoder.
The Kompensation template will help to ensure good settings for Encoder Distance per Rotation, Angle of Laser, and Dir -
ection of Product Movement. See "Kompensation Template" below .
16. Adjust Parameter settings to get a good mark quality using the customer production line running at production speed.
17. Configure the IP address for Ethernet port (s), if necessary. You can set two IP addresses through the Web Interface (see
"Edit Laser Configuration" on page 408 ).
Kompensation Template
The purpose of the Kompensation Test is to fine tune the Distance Per Rotation and Angle Between the Laser and Direction of
Product Movement in the Product Registration. The test is highly recommended for applications involving a 2D data matrix bar
code, but should be used in most installations to achieve most accurate results on setup of encoder.
The following procedure is described using the SmartGraph software.
Importing the Kompensation Template
1. Download the Kompensation.tpl template to a location on your computer.
You can find the Kompensation template file in the VTI Support & Technology team space.
2. Launch SmartGraph .
3. Connect to the Laser in the File tab. Ensure that after connection, the Laser side (right hand side) of the File tab is high -
lighted. The highlighted side is where the imported file will be saved.
4. Click Import .
5. Select the Import File option from the drop- down menu.
278
Rev AF

Installation
6. Browse to the Kompensation template on your computer. Ensure that the Type of File in the bottom of the window is
set to look for Templates (*.tpl).
7. Select the template and click Open .
8. Verify that the Kompensation template now appears in the list of Templates on the right hand side of screen in the File
tab.
Setting the Product Registration
1. Ensure that the Laser side of File tab is active, indicated by an orange banner above the files section.
2. Click the Configuration tab.
3. Click the Marking Head tab.
279
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
4. Set the Marking Head orientation based on the orientation of the marking head relative to the product. A small picture
will show the orientation of the head based on your choice. Adjust until the orientation matches the mounting of the laser.
5. Click Apply .
6. Click the Product Registration tab .
7. Click New to create a new product registration.
8. Name your file and click OK.
9. Make the necessary adjustment to your product registration base on Trigger type, Start Distance, and Blocking.
10. Enter twice the pulses per rotation of your encoder. For example, if a 2000 pulse per rotation encoder enter 4000 pulses in
the Product Registration.
11. Enter the diameter of encoder wheel (if present) in the Pulses Per Rotation box.
12. Enter the Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement . Use the chart below to assist in assessing the
setting for both Mounting Position and Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement (abbreviated Product
Movement Angle).
280
Rev AF

Installation
Setting the Parameters
The Kompensation template imports with a Parameter set called AP08111318.
Product Movement Too Fast Faults
Depending on the conveyor line speed you may have to adjust the parameters to avoid Product Movement
Too Fast faults.
281
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Adjust the Marking Speed to a speed fast enough to avoid Product Movement Too Fast errors, but slow enough to create a mark
on the product.
Printing the Kompensation Template
1. Click on the Marking tab.
2. Click on the Marking Adjustment tab.
3. Select the Kompensation template from the drop- down list.
4. Click on the Marking tab.
282
Rev AF

Installation
5. Press the Start button.
6. The laser status should indicate Waiting for Trigger. If this is not displayed, check that you have permissions to fire the
laser and that the Trigger Enable input has 24 volts applied (often jumpered X25.9- X25.12).
See "Permissions" on page 405 .
7. As the product passes through the product detector you should see the Marking indication. If not, adjust the Start
Distance in your Product Registration.
Troubleshooting
To center the product in the print area, adjust the Start Delay in Product Registration.
If message is unreadable, check the Angle of Product Movement in Product Registration.
If message is of correct orientation but of poor quality, adjust the Parameters.
Understanding the Results
If both the Distance Per Rotation in encoder setting and Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement are correct
then the mark from the Kompensation template should be a cross with no curves on either axis as shown in Figure 9- 1 below .
F IGURE 9- 1: A mark indicating correct Distance Per Rotation and Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement are
both correct.
Make adjustments to correct for curvature in the horizontal or vertical axis.
283
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Setting the Correct Mounting Position
Once the correct Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement is found, it may be necessary to
adjust the Mounting Position in the Marking Head tab. If conveyor line direction is left to right, then the Mount -
ing Position should be adjusted to the same number as the Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product
Movement. If conveyor direction is right to left, then Mounting position should be adjusted to 180 degree off
from Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement.
284
Rev AF

1. Connect encoder, product detect, and switch for interlock.
2. Adjust Mounting Position to correct head orientation.
3. Create Product Registration called SG Install with the following attributes:
l Fires once when Start is pressed and then stops firing
l No product movement.

Maintenance Schedule
The 3340/3140 /3640 requires a relatively low amount of maintenance, but the environment in which the laser operates can
have a significant effect on how often maintenance tasks must be completed.
Recommended maintenance intervals are based on laser system use of approximately 10 hours daily operation in a normal work -
ing environment. If daily use exceeds 10 hours of continuous operation, or in case of very dusty/dirty environments, it may be
necessary to clean the laser tube or filters or fume extractor filters more frequently.
Possible Injury or Equipment Damage
Only trained personnel should carry out the installation and maintenance work. Any such work undertaken by
unauthorized personnel can damage the printer and invalidate the warranty.
Personal Injury
Before the beginning of the cleaning works of the laser system and its environment, the laser system must be
disconnected from the power supply.
Symptoms indicating maintenance is needed include:
l Faint marking (dirty lens)
l Overheating fault (dirty laser tube)
