Lens Cleaning Procedure
The laser scan lens is located at the marking head. It may become contaminated by dust or floating particles. Contamination of
the laser scan lens can lead to damage of the laser scan lens and will gradually reduce the quality of the marking. You should
therefore clean the lens regularly.
Generally just the outwardly facing side of the laser scan lens requires cleaning, but check both sides for contamination and clean
as necessary.
289
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Personal Injury - Zinc Selenide
The laser scan lens consists of coated zinc selenide. This material is hazardous. Avoid contact and take care to
not damage the lens, which may release the zinc selenide as a particulate.
To prevent personal injury when handling these substances, always wear protective clothing and rubber gloves.
In case of contact with the laser scan lens with the skin, wash the respective part of skin with water and soap
immediately. Avoid any surface scratches of the laser scan lens. Do not inhale material particles. In the event
of a broken laser scan lens, carefully pack the fragments into a closed plastic bag and return to for disposal.
Lens Damage
As with all optical components the laser scan lens is an object of highest precision and sophisticated design!
Slightest damages of the surface might (in the long term) result in unusability of the component or reduction
of marking quality.
Avoid any surface scratches of the laser scan lens. Remove any contaminants using an optics cleaning cloth
and isopropyl alcohol or acetone (included in the accessory box).
Ensure that no contaminants enter the marking head while cleaning the laser scan lens!
Required Supplies
l Optics cleaning paper
l Acetone (methanol or isopropyl alcohol also acceptable)
l Protective gloves
Clean the Scan Lens
Personal Injury - Shock Hazard Warning
Before starting any work the laser system must be dead!
Personal InjuryPersonal Injury
To prevent personal injury when handling these substances, always wear protective clothing and rubber gloves.
1. Turn the key switch to position 0 .
2. Remove the key to ensure that the laser system cannot be switched on.
3. Switch off the main switch.
4. Disconnect the mains connector.
290
Rev AF

Maintenance Schedule
5. Visually inspect the marking head scan lens. If possible to clean the lens without removing the lens from marking head
leave the lens on the marking head. Leaving the scan lens on the marking head protects from introduction of dust/debris
to interior components of marking head as shown below.
If after cleaning the exterior, or if not possible to clean scan lens without removing from marking head, then remove lens
using the following method:
6. Remove the Marking Head. Note that the mounting of lenses for each model differ slightly.
Possible Equipment Damage
Do not touch the lens surface!
SHC 60C Marking Head
6.1. Turn the laser scan lens carefully by a quarter turn in counterclockwise direction.
6.2. Remove the laser scan lens from the marking head.
SHC 100C/120C/150C Marking Heads
6.1. Remove the two fixing screws from the laser scan lens holder.
6.2. Remove the laser scan lens from the marking head. Do not touch the surface of lens with your glove.
7. Place the laser scan lens onto a clean surface.
Avoid damage to the lens
l Never use polishing agents on the laser lens. Polishing agents will destroy the laser scan lens.
l Never use contaminated optics cleaning paper.
l Make sure that the optics cleaning paper is stored in a clean place.
8. Fold a sheet of optics cleaning paper at least 5 times to create a flat cushion.
291
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
9. Hold the optics cleaning paper between thumb and index finger and put a few drops of acetone onto the optics clean -
ing paper.
10. Carefully and slowly pull the optics cleaning paper across the surface to be cleaned working from center to outer edge.
l Make sure that you do no touch the surface with gloves!
l Apply only very light pressure onto the paper!
11. If necessary, repeat the procedure with a new sheet of optics cleaning paper.
12. Check the surface of the lens side located in the marking head for contamination. If necessary, clean the inner side of lens
as described above.
If the lens cannot be cleaned or the surface of the protective glass is severely scratched, replace the laser scan lens.
Remount the Scan Lens
For SHC 60C
1. Insert the laser scan lens into the marking head.
2. Carefully turn the laser scan lens clockwise by a quarter turn. Do not use any tools .
For SHC 100C/120C/150C
1. Insert the laser scan lens into the marking head.
2. Mount the laser scan lens using the two fixing screws.
292
Rev AF

Maintenance Schedule
Supply Unit Filters
Supply units which use fans for cooling have two grills that contain filter mats behind them. The filter mats must be exchanged
when they become clogged with debris. They should be inspected monthly and exchanged as necessary. Ambient conditions and
number of hours/days the laser is used greatly affect the frequency with which the filter mats must be exchanged. Symptoms of
clogged filter mats include system lock ups. IP65 laser units use a blower unit for cooling rather than internal fans. The blower
unit will have its own internal filter/filters that must be exchanged when clogged as well (see blower unit manual for instructions).
Exchanging the Filter Mats
1. Turn the key switch to position 0 .
2. Remove the key to ensure that the laser system cannot be switched on.
3. Switch off the main switch.
4. Disconnect the mains connector.
5. Open the grills at the side and at the back of the supply unit by inserting a narrow screw driver into the groove from below
and levering carefully.
6. Remove the filter mats. Dispose of the mats according to the local regulations.
7. Insert new mats.
8. Close the grills.
293
Rev AF

Troubleshooting
First Steps
When troubleshooting a no mark issue or always begin by:
l Clean output lens (See "Lens Cleaning Procedure" on page 289 ).
l Ensure Working Distance (See "Working Distance" on page 174 ) is correct for head/lens combination (marking head to
product distance).
l Checking that product detect and encoder are connected and in serviceable condition.
l Ensure umbilical and other cables are in serviceable condition.
l Switch laser ON.
l Look up any Error messages
l Check Product Registration/Line Setup
l Verify desired Product Registration is assigned to Template/Job
l Verify the settings in the Product Registration
l Verify the Configuration settings (mark head type, lens type)
l Fire in static mode to decide if laser is capable of marking when no peripherals involved
l SmartGraph Product Registration set to No Product Movement and Triggers set to Auto and None
l TCS/Clarity Line Setup set to Static product movement and Internal Trigger
295
Rev AF

Troubleshooting

Troubleshooting
Start Sequence
Following is the start- up sequence for the 3340/3140 /3640 .
Shock Hazard Warning
48 Volts are always present at the Chameleon board unless system is unplugged.
1. Press On/Standby button.
2. K1 relay on Chameleon board operates.
3. CPD3 (3140/3340) or SPM (3640) board operates.
l Diagnostic LEDs on board begin flashing.
4. White LED on On/Standby button comes on solid.
5. Voltage LEDs on Chameleon board light.
l 24Vdc on to Supply Unit fan (IP54 only)
6. White LED on On/Standby button starts to flash.
l Software loads
7. Chameleon board IC72 turns on 48V to CP- Light.
l Voltage LEDs on CP- Light board lit.
8. Communication with CP- Light board established.
l Red Comms LED on CP- Light board goes out.
9. Identifies position of mirrors (shutter).
l DC1 on HIC is lit.
10. White light on On/Standby button stops flashing and stays on solid.
Changing Fuses 3140/3340
There are two fuses located in the power cord receptacle on rear of the supply unit. Symptoms of fuse failure includes inability to
turn on power to laser unit. These fuses are not present in the 3640 laser model.
Remove Fuse Holder
Shock Hazard Warning
Lethal voltages are present within this equipment when it is connected to the mains electrical supply. Before
starting any work power the laser system OFF and completely disconnected from mains power supply.
343
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
1. Remove four screws that hold cover over power cord receptacle on rear of supply unit.
2. Remove power cord from receptacle.
3. Apply pressure onto the holder of the fuse insert (see figure) and pull out the insert.
4. Remove the fuses. Dispose of the fuses according to the local regulations.
344
Rev AF

Troubleshooting
5. Insert new fuses (2 x T8A) and put in the insert.
6. Mount the cover.
7. Replace power cord and cover and tighten four screws holding cover.
Laser System Will Not Boot
l Symptom: White light on Power/Standby button flashes as boot process starts, but does not stop flashing after 5 minutes or
more.
l Symptom: When the Power/Standby button is pressed to start the boot process of the laser the white light comes on solid,
but does not start flashing.
Initial Steps
Before troubleshooting boot issues, it is necessary to determine which variant of Chameleon board is present: Single Core
Chameleon or Multi- Core Chameleon. Proceed to the appropriate section below based on this identification.
You can distinguish the two boards by looking at the Q7 module and board color. A Q7 with silver pins on the heat sink denotes a
Single Core Chameleon while a Q7 with a black linear heat sink (fins) is present on both the Multi- Core Chameleon board and
Multi- Core Industrial Protocols board. The main board of the Multi- Core Industrial Protocols Chameleon is additionally black in
color and also has a connector for the optional MIC board between the Q7 and the dip switches. This connector is not present on
the green Multi- Core board.
345
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
F IGURE 11- 3: Q7 circled. (Left) A Single Core Chameleon. (Center) A Multi- Core Chameleon. (Right) A Multi- Core Industrial Pro -
tocols Chameleon.
Single Core Chameleon Board Diagnostics
1. Did you wait long enough? There is an approximate lag time of 20 seconds before the white light starts to flash. Approx -
imately 5 minutes will elapse after the white light starts to flash until it remains on solidly.
2. Is the battery attached to the Chameleon board? Check battery voltage. Minimum voltage is 2.7 volts.
3. Is the SD card seated correctly? If not:
3.1. Power main power OFF (no lights on keypad) or Standby mode (blue light on keypad).
3.2. Reseat the card.
3.3. Reconnect power.
3.4. Attempt to reboot.
4. Reload software on the SD card.
Preparing an SD Card using XMarkCopy Tool
If using an SD card that has never had CMark installed through XMarkCopy Tool, or if card did not
come from Videojet loaded with CMark, then it is necessary to load CMark operating system through
the XMarkCopy Tool. Use of this software tool to load CMark installs a hidden bootloader file that is
necessary on the SD card. Once CMark has been loaded onto an SD card via the XMarkCopy Tool it is
not necessary in the future and CMark loading can be done also through Laser Web Interface or by
manually extracting files to the card. If status of the card is not known, please use XMarkCopy Tool to
load CMark. If the hidden bootloader file has not been installed the outcome is that the laser will not
boot.
5. If the reload fails, try again using a new SD card with a clean version of CMark. "Clean version of CMark" means no cus -
tomer files are included.
346
Rev AF

Troubleshooting
6. Is there 48V sent from Chameleon board to CP- Light?
l Measure at X28.12 (48V ) and X28.6 (Ground) - If no voltage at X28.12 then verify wiring at X28. If correct, replace
Chameleon board.
l Measure 48V at power connector on CP- Light board - If no voltage at the CP- Light power connector, replace umbil -
ical.
7. If the white light is on, but fails to start/stop flashing after diagnostics complete, replace the Chameleon board.
Multi- Core Chameleon Diagnostics
1. Did you wait long enough? There is an approximate lag time of 20 seconds before the white light starts to flash. Approx -
imately 5 minutes will elapse after the white light starts to flash until it remains on solidly.
2. Is the battery attached to the Chameleon board? Does the battery produce at least 2.7 volts (minimum voltage for boot)?
3. Are there 48V sent from Chameleon board to CP- Light?
l Measure at X28.12 (48V) and X28.6 (Ground) during boot. If there is no voltage at X28.12, verify wiring at X28. If wir -
ing is correct, replace the Chameleon board. If voltage is present at X28, then proceed to step 4.
l Measure 48V at power connector on CP- Light board during boot - If no voltage at CP- Light power connector replace
umbilical.
4. Unplug the laser system and insert a Rescue Stick USB drive with a rescue image file into USB port on Chameleon. This
image is more than just the CMark operating system.
l If corrupted files on the Chameleon board are the issue causing boot to fail, overwriting the image will allow the
laser to boot after the rescue process is complete. It is necessary to connect to the Web Interface and load
CMark through the Web Interface if the system boots successfully.
Possible Data Loss - Full System Rewrite
Use a Rescue Stick only as a last resort if corruption of system files is suspected. The main symp -
tom of corrupted system files is an inability to boot the system successfully. If a Rescue Stick is
inserted into the USB port and the laser is booted, all existing customer files will be wiped from
memory and overwritten with the factory default image. All customer files and CMark will be
lost. The system database is not erased (Laser type/Marking Head type/Lens/Serial
Number/IP addresses).
5. If the laser fails to boot following use of Rescue Stick, replace the Chameleon board.
The Chameleon Rescue Stick
A Rescue Stick is a USB memory stick prepared with a CMark image that allows you to recover a failed laser. You can create a res -
cue stick and carry it with you as a service tool.
347
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Minimum Required Version
CMark versions 4.0 and later support the Rescue Stick feature. You cannot recover single core Chameleon
boards with the rescue stick, only multi- core boards.
Use a Rescue Stick:
l When a laser running CMark from the Flash memory will not boot.
l When a laser will not finish the boot cycle, preventing you from reloading CMark via the Web Interface.
l Before replacing the Chameleon to resolve failure to boot. A Rescue Stick will overwrite system files on the Chameleon,
allowing the laser to successfully boot if corrupted system files were the root cause.
It is suggested that you create a rescue stick before you need it and carry it as service tool. It takes approximately 45 minutes to
download the rescue image and write the files to USB.
When a prepared Rescue Stick is inserted into the USB slot on a Chameleon board, it will automatically overwrite anything in the
Chameleon Board Flash Memory. This will completely erase all files on Chameleon and set it to the manufacturer default
state with the exception of the system database . After this rewrite process, it is necessary to connect to the Web Interface to
load CMark. CMark is not included as part of the rescue image. After loading CMark, you should verify the Laser Configuration in
the Web Interface.
Possible Data Loss - Full System Rewrite
Use a Rescue Stick only as a last resort if corruption of system files is suspected. The main symptom of cor -
rupted system files is an inability to boot the system successfully. If a Rescue Stick is inserted into the USB port
and the laser is booted, all existing customer files will be wiped from memory and overwritten with the factory
default image. All customer files and CMark will be lost. The system database is not erased (Laser type/Mark -
ing Head type/Lens/Serial Number/IP addresses).
Creating a Rescue Stick
The following procedure details installing the Rescue Image to a USB memory stick.
1. Download both CMark_ Rescue_ 1.x.img and HDD Raw Copy Tool.exe . Contact Videojet Technical Support if you do not
have access to the download locations.

4. Click on FILE  from the list of sources.
5. Browse to and select the Rescue Image file you downloaded.
6. Click Continue .

Troubleshooting
Recover Multi- Core Chameleon with a
Rescue Stick
This process only applies to Multi- Core Chameleon boards that will not successfully boot. This procedure will not recover a single
core Chameleon. Recovery of single core Chameleon boards that will not boot should be attempted through reloading of the SD
card with CMark or replacement of the SD card.
Laser Will Be Set Back to Factory Image
When a laser system is booted with a Rescue Image USB stick inserted into the USB slot on the Chameleon
board, it will wipe all files on the laser's flash storage. Those files are then irretrievable. The desired CMark must
be available for installation after Rescue Image is installed in order for the laser to function correctly.
The System Database, which is stored on EEPROM on Chameleon, will not be lost. This contains the laser type,
head type, lens, serial number and other hardware configuration items.
1. Insert the USB stick with the Rescue Image into USB port on the Chameleon board of the laser. For instructions on transfer
of Rescue Image to USB see "Creating a Rescue Stick" on page 348 .
2. Ensure the laser system is connected to power before proceeding. The blue light on the membrane keypad should be lit.
3. Press the On/Standby button on the membrane keypad of the laser to boot the laser. The white light will come on dimly
and eventually start to flash as the image on the USB memory stick begins to load. The laser system will shut down
when the image load is complete. Approximate time to completion is 5- 10 minutes.
If the Rescue Image loading was successful, the laser system should boot when powered on and will allow you to con -
nect to the Web Interface to continue. You must load CMark before checking the system configuration .
The only part of system that is not overwritten is the EEPROM. The EEPROM stores the system configuration including IP
addresses, laser type, head type, lens type, and serial number. Even though the EEPROM is not overwritten, check
these for accuracy after loading CMark or CMark Backup.
Troubleshooting: Laser still won't boot
If the laser system still will not boot, replace the Chameleon board.
4. Install the desired CMark version or CMark Backup.
Installing a fresh install of CMark or a CMark backup are achieved through the same steps. For detailed information on
backup creation see "CMark Backup" on page 418 . Following are generalized steps for loading either CMark or a CMark
backup
Generalized steps to install CMark or CMark backup

connect to 192.168.1.1 with dip switch 1 in the On position to find the configured primary IP in the laser Web Inter -
face at Laser Menu | Configuration.
4.3. Set DIP Switch 1 to OFF if you used it to determine the primary IP Address.
4.4. Connect to the laser via the primary IP address.
Use Internet Explorer version 9 or newer only for CMark Update or any other file transfer.
The Web Interface branding will be Alltec Foba until after CMark is loaded.

the laser Configuration through the Web Interface before any other actions can be performed.

Chameleon" on the facing page .

part.

Upgrade from Single Core to Multi- Core
Chameleon
If desired, the Single Core Chameleon which stores CMark and customer files on an SD card can be replaced with a Multi- Core
Chameleon board which stores this information in Flash memory on the Chameleon's Q7 module. This upgrade is necessary if
TCS+ is required. TCS+ is only available with Multi- Core Chameleon boards.
Identifying the Board Type
You can distinguish the two boards by looking at the Q7 module and board color. A Q7 with silver pins on the heat sink denotes a
Single Core Chameleon while a Q7 with a black linear heat sink (fins) is present on both the Multi- Core Chameleon board and
357
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Multi- Core Industrial Protocols board. The main board of the Multi- Core Industrial Protocols Chameleon is additionally black in
color and also has a connector for the optional MIC board between the Q7 and the dip switches. This connector is not present on
the green Multi- Core board.
F IGURE 11- 4: Q7 circled. (Left) A Single Core Chameleon. (Center) A Multi- Core Chameleon. (Right) A Multi- Core Industrial Pro -
tocols Chameleon.

Marking Head Faults
The following faults are considered marking head faults where the CP- Light board is either not transmitting or receiving signals
from the galvos or the Chameleon board.
Symptom: Laser boots, but any of the following errors occur:
l E00213- Wrong HIC detected
l E09022 - Internal error: Cannot set configuration mode of the marking head
l E09023 - Internal error: Cannot set download mode of the marking head
l E09100 - No communication with marking head
362
Rev AF

Troubleshooting
l E09102 - Interrupted communication with marking head
l E09104 - No data received from the marking head
Diagnostics
1. Connect to laser Interface and verify that laser model and marking head type are set correctly. See "Connect to the Web
Interface" on page 398 for connection instructions.
l If model or marking head type are not correct, log in to the Web Interface and change to correct type. Reboot system
after making change.
l If model and marking head type are correct continue to step 2.
2. Are LEDs present on CP- Light?
l If no LEDs, check for 48V at power connector on CP- Light. If no voltage, verify 48V out at X28 pins 6 and 12.
l If LEDs are lit, continue to next step.
3. Verify all pins on umbilical connector at rear or marking head are making good contact and are not bent. Look closely at
pins to identify if any on male side of connector have pushed back in toward crimp/solder point on rear of connector.
4. Check continuity of LVDS cable (CAT- 7 cable).
5. Check HIC board.
Marking Head Errors After Galvo
Replacement
Symptom: The following errors are displayed after replacing galvo/CP- Light of older generation with newer generation parts.
Example: Replacing Marking Head Type C components with Type D or Type E versions or Type D with Type E.
l E09100: No communication with marking head
l E10519: Please select a lens corresponding to the marking head
l E10509: Error while reading the head file
363
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Solution
If these errors occur after changing galvo/CP- Light on SHC60 or SHC100 marking heads to new style galvos/CP- Light2/4, please
update CMark to version 3.4.1.2 or newer (Type D) or CMark 4.9.2 or newer (Type E).
Type D galvos/CP- Light2 cannot function without errors when the installed version of CMark is older than 3.4.1.2. Type E gal -
vos/CP- Light4 cannot function without errors when version of CMark is older than 4.9.2.
You can identify the generation of galvos/CP- Light by comparing the combination of physical components of the galvo block,
marking head cable connector color and CP- Light board color. The combination of colors and physical traits of galvo block are
unique to each generation.

Shutter Faults
Shutter faults occur when the galvos do not close the mechanical shutter switch that identifies them as being in the closed pos -
ition. The faults that may occur are:
l E10302 - Shutter does not stay open
l E10303 - Shutter does not stay closed
Overview
Shutter faults can be caused either by the galvos not moving to the position which would close the electromechanical shutter
(failure to move) or by the Chameleon not receiving the shutter closed information (failure to relay information).
Components
The following components are involved in the correct function of the shutter system.
l CP- Light
l Galvos
l Marking Head Cable
l Laser Head Harness
364
Rev AF

Troubleshooting
l Umbilical
l HIC
l Chameleon
Diagnostics
1. Verify that shutter and LVDS (CAT 7 cable) connectors are seated correctly at HIC and CP- Light boards.
2. Verify all pins on umbilical connector at rear or marking head are making good contact and are not bent. Look closely at
pins to identify if any on male side of connector have pushed back in toward crimp/solder point on rear of connector.
3. Check continuity of shutter cable. Wire colors and pin numbers are shown in the following figure.
4. Check connectors at HIC and on CP- Light boards. Both LVDS and Shutter cables seated correctly?
5. Remove marking head lens and cover. With unit off, gently move the galvos slightly from their current position. Power unit
on and watch to see if the galvos move.
6. If galvos do not move suspect failure in current supply to galvos (HIC, CP- Light, LVDS, marking head cable, or galvos).
If you suspect that the LVDS Cable  is broken, you can test by connecting a straight through (i.e. not a
crossover) Ethernet cable from the HIC board in the supply unit to CP- Light board to bypass umbilical. If the shutter fault
is resolved, the umbilical must be replaced. See Figure 11- 5 on the next page of the Ethernet port on the HIC board. The
network cable on the umbilical is Cat7, but a straight through Cat5 is sufficient for testing.
365
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
F IGURE 11- 5: The umbilical Cat7 cable plugged in to the port on the HIC board.
If galvos do move , then likely the issue is with shutter return signal (Shutter cable, marking head cable, HIC,
Chameleon).
Laser Tube Faults
The following faults are considered laser tube faults in which the laser tube either doesn't receive power or signals or returns sig -
nals indicating an error condition.
Symptom: Key switch turned and any of the laser tube faults below is displayed.
l E00200 - Laser Not Ready
l E00201 - Laser Supply: Voltage Too High or Too Low
l E00204 - General Error
l E00211 - Defective Element in Laser Supply Circuit
l E00212 - Wrong LIC Detected
l E00214 - Tube Monitoring Error
Overview of Laser On Steps
When key switch is closed, assuming that the interlock/shutterlock is closed, the following happens:
1. Mains voltage applied to 30V laser source power supply via relays 1, 2, 3, and 4 on SPM board (relays 1, 2, and 3 on CPD
board).
2. 30V dc* (48V dc for Vi30 systems) output from laser source power supply. (*actual voltage dependent on umbilical length)
366
Rev AF

Troubleshooting
3. 30V dc* (48V dc for Vi30 systems) returned to X28.13 and X28.14 on Chameleon board.
4. 30V dc* (48V dc for Vi30 systems) returned to X9 on SPM/CPD3.
5. LEDs are lit on LIC_ CO2 board.
6. Fan in laser head comes on (IP54 only).
7. 30V dc* (48V dc for Vi30 systems) to laser source in laser head (30V/48V at tube).
8. Emission LEDs come on.
9. System goes to Laser Ready state.
Diagnostics
1. Verify Interlock/Shutterlock are closed.
2. Verify the laser model in Web Interface. Correct in Web Interface if necessary.
See "Edit Laser Configuration" on page 408 .
3. Verify jumper between X.33.2- 6 is in place and making good contact. Failure of this jumper can result in E00201: Laser Sup -
ply Voltage Too High or Too Low.
4. Measure dc voltage power supply output at Terminal Block and adjust if necessary. (See "Adjusting Laser Power Supply
Voltage" on page 373 ). The following voltages should be observed at Terminal Block (+V and Laser Ground). If no
voltage adjustment is possible replace laser power supply (laser power supply). Ensure connection at Terminal Block is
secure and correct before replacing parts.
Table 11- 5: Expected Voltages at Terminal Block
Volts dc at Terminal Block Volts dc at Terminal Block
Umbilical
3140/3340 with V30 laser tube 3340 with Vi30 laser tube
3 meter umbilical 31.0 (+/- 0.3V) 48.5 (+/- 0.3V)
5 meter umbilical 31.5 (+/- 0.3V) 48.7 (+/- 0.3V)
10 meter umbilical 32.0 (+/- 0.3V) 49.1 (+/- 0.3V)
5. If no voltage measured at Terminal Block, verify input voltage (AC) to laser power supply. If no voltage at input suspect
SPM/CPD3 board.
6. Check voltage at the laser tube. 3140/3340 with V30 laser tube models should show 30V at the laser tube connection.
3340 with Vi30 laser tube models should show 48V at the laser tube connection. If voltage is present, replace laser tube. If
voltage is not present, proceed to the next step.
7. Check continuity of voltage and signal cables from the supply unit to connectors in the laser head. If an open is found,
verify whether the short is between the supply unit and umbilical connector on the rear of the laser head (replace umbilical)
or from the umbilical connector on the rear of laser head to the connector in the laser head (replace laser head internal har -
ness).
367
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training

Laser Tube Faults (3640)
The following faults are considered laser tube faults in which the laser tube either doesn't receive power or signals or returns sig -
nals indicating an error condition.
Symptom: Key switch turned and any of the laser tube faults below is displayed.
l E00200 - Laser Not Ready
l E00201 - Laser Supply: Voltage Too High or Too Low
l E00204 - General Error
l E00211 - Defective Element in Laser Supply Circuit
l E00212 - Wrong LIC Detected
l E00214 - Tube Monitoring Error
Overview of Laser On Steps
When key switch is closed, assuming that the interlock/shutterlock is closed, the following happens:
1. Mains voltage applied to 48V laser source power supply (50V dc) via relays 1, 2, 3, and 4 on board.
2. 50V dc output from laser source power supply.
3. 50V dc returned to X28.13 and X28.14 on Chameleon board.
4. 50V dc returned to X9 on SPM.
5. LEDs are lit on LIC_ CO2 board.
6. Fan in laser head comes on (IP54 only).
7. 50V dc to laser source in laser head (50V at tube).
8. Emission LEDs come on.
9. System goes to Laser Ready state.
Diagnostics
1. Verify Interlock/Shutterlock are closed.
2. Verify laser model in Web Interface. Correct in Web Interface if necessary.
See "Edit Laser Configuration" on page 408 .
3. Verify jumper between X.33.2- 6 is in place and making good contact. Failure of this jumper can result in E00201: Laser Sup -
ply Voltage Too High or Too Low.
4. Measure dc voltage power supply output at Terminal Block , 50V dc should be observed at Terminal Block (50V and Laser
Ground terminals). Ensure connection at Terminal Block is secure and correct before replacing parts.
l If no voltage is measured at the Terminal Block, verify input voltage (AC) to the laser power supply. If no voltage is
present, suspect SPM board.
l If input (AC) voltage is present but there is no output, then replace laser power supply.
370
Rev AF

Troubleshooting
5. Check voltage at the laser tube. This should read 50V at the laser tube power connection. If voltage is present, replace the
laser tube. If voltage is not present, proceed to the next step.
6. Check the continuity of voltage and signal cables from the supply unit to connectors in the laser head. If an open is found,
verify whether the short is between the supply unit and the umbilical connector on the rear of the laser head (replace umbil -
ical) or from the umbilical connector on the rear of the laser head to the connector in the laser head (replace laser head
internal harness).
371
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
F IGURE 11- 8: Diagram: 3640 Wiring diagram laser power and signals from LIC to 60 Watt laser tube.
372
Rev AF

Troubleshooting
Adjusting Laser Power Supply Voltage
The output of the laser power supply on 3140/3640 lasers should be adjusted based on the length of the umbilical connected to
the laser. This adjustment, or at least the measurement of output voltage, should be done as part of troubleshooting suspected
laser tube issues and also after a new laser power supply is installed. Laser power supplies ordered as spare parts for the
3140/3340 come set out of specification and must be adjusted or faults will occur. The 3640 laser does not require this adjust -
ment and the laser power supply for 3640 lasers cannot be accessed once power supply is installed in the laser.
Shock Hazard Warning
Lethal voltages are present within this equipment when it is connected to the mains electrical supply. During
this procedure live components are accessible. Make sure that rules and regulations for work on live com -
ponents are always followed. All work at the open laser, especially at electrical components, must only be per -
formed by specially trained personnel!
Tools Required
l Multimeter
l Large flat- blade screwdriver
l Potentiometer adjustment tool (AL- 76378)
Measure Laser Power Supply Voltage
1. Boot the laser system.
2. Open the supply unit cover.
3. Turn the laser key switch to the ON position. The laser tube must be powered in order to read voltage.
4. Measure DC voltage at connector X9 on SPM/CPD. The white wire (pin 3) is positive and gray wire (pin 13) is ground.
Measurement can also be accomplished at pin 13 and pin 14 on X28 connector on Chameleon or at the terminal block
at + Laser and GND Laser terminals by removing black cap on terminal.
373
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Voltages should be within the following ranges:
10 Watt Laser Tubes and V30 Laser Tubes (does not apply to Vi30 30 Watt Laser Tubes)
l 3m: 31.0V (+/- 0.3V)
l 5m: 31.5V (+/- 0.3V)
l 10m: 32.0V (+/- 0.3V)
30 Watt Vi30 Laser Tubes
l 3m: 48.5V (+/- 0.3V)
l 5m: 48.7V (+/- 0.3V)
l 10m: 49.1V (+/- 0.3V)
5. If measured voltage is not within the expected range, adjust the laser power voltage.

Troubleshooting
Safety Preparation
Before proceeding with the power measurement, read, understand, and implement the following safety precautions.
Warning - Serious Personal Injury
Direct or diffuse laser radiation can inflict severe bodily damage , including, but not limited to, permanent eye
damage or blindness, and severe burns to human or animal tissue.  Always wear the appropriate eye pro -
tection when in the same area as an operating laser. Do not allow a direct or reflected beam to contact a per -
son .
Diligence and awareness are essential to prevent the beam from traveling beyond the intended work area.
Warning - Serious Personal Injury
When performing a power measurement, it is the responsibility of the person performing the power meas -
urement to  enclose and protect the surrounding area and personnel from the laser beam and, when possible,
perform the power measurement within the laser enclosure / beam shield.
Warning - Serious Personal Injury
When performing a power measurement, the use of a Carbon Block is required , placed behind the power
meter to absorb the laser energy when misalignments occur.
Preparing the Power Measurement Template
1. Download the template archive if you have not already.
2. Extract the files from the archive to a location on your PC/Laptop.
3. Connect your PC/laptop to the laser using the Ethernet cable.
4. If Windows is not already configured to be on the same network subnet as the laser, adjust those settings now.
5. Open Smartgraph and connect to the laser system.
6. Click to select the Laser (right) pane.
7. Import the Power Check.tpl  template file, the Power Check Product Reg.prd Product Registration file, and the Power Check
Parameter Set.par parameter set file.
8. Import the Synrad Power Meter.tpl Template file, the Synrad Power Meter Template.par parameter file, and the Synrad
Power Meter.prd Product Registration file.
9. Click to select the Template tab.
10. Open the Synrad Power Check in the template tab.
11. Click on  Parameters  icon.
12. Set the  Marking Intensity  to 20% for the initial setup. You will adjust this again later for the power measurement.
379
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
13. Set the remaining parameters to the appropriate setting as shown in Table 11- 7 below .
Table 11- 7: Table 2: Power CheckTemplate Parameters
CO2 (all)
Jump Speed 15,000 mm/s
Marking Speed 1 mm/s
Jump Delay 50 us
Mark Delay 0 us
Stroke Delay 50 us
Marking Intensity 20 %
Pulse Frequency 15,000 Hz
On Delay 0 us
Off Delay 50 us
14. Click Apply to save the changes and then Close .
Measurement Notes/Guidelines
l It is permissible to take multiple power readings in succession, but allow time for the meter to cool after 2- 3 measurements.
l Do not cool the Power Wizard by immersing it in water. Allow for air cooling or place in front of a fan.
l Use care to place the beam on the target, rather than the Power Wizard case. Move the Power Wizard in and out of the
beam quickly without letting the beam dwell on the case. Never press the Reset/Power button while the target is exposed to
the laser beam, the computer will not initialize properly.
l NEVER place the Power Wizard at the focal point of the lens!!
l Erroneous readings may indicate on or more the following conditions:
l The Power Wizard was not reset for at least two seconds
l The target is not being held stationary in the beam path
l The beam is not completely within the target area
l Laser power is fluctuating too rapidly
l The Power Wizard is malfunctioning.

Rev AF

Troubleshooting
Prepare the Laser Unit, Meter and Area for the Power Meas -
urement
Eye and Skin Hazard
Direct or diffuse laser radiation can inflict severe bodily damage, including, but not limited to permanent eye
damage or blindness, and severe burns to human or animal tissue. Always wear appropriate eye protection
when in the same area as an operating laser. Do not allow a direct or reflected beam to contact a person.
Always be aware of the laser’s beam path when using a laser power meter and always use a beam block to pre -
vent the beam from traveling beyond the intended work area.
1. Ensure that all personnel in the area are wearing the appropriate protective eye wear.
2. Ensure the exposed laser beam will remain enclosed during the test.
l If the laser application already uses an enclosure, perform the test with the laser head inside its enclosure.
l If the laser is not enclosed already in the application, place non flammable materials around the laser head to
enclose it during the test. Use materials appropriate for blocking the wavelength in use.
3. Place the carbon block in below or in front of the marking head in the beam path: horizontally below a downward aimed
marking head or vertically in front of a forward aimed marking head.
4. Tape a piece of laser card stock (PN: SAR20049004) to the carbon block.
5. Position the marking head such that the card is  not  at the working focal distance - preferably the distance should be
between 60% and 80% of the actual working focal distance for the installed lens.
Perform the Power Measurement
This procedure will require actions in quick succession. Read the following steps in their entirety before beginning the power test .
1. In Smartgraph, open the Synrad Power Meter Template in the Template tab.
2. If necessary, rotate the template to accommodate the orientation of the laser head.
3. Select the Configuration Tab and select the Product Registration subtab.
381
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
4. Ensure Synrad Power Meter Template file name is shown at the left in the Product Registration tab and that the fol -
lowing values are set:
l Start Trigger : Auto
l Consecutive Trigger : None
l Registration of product Movement : No product Movement
5. Turn the key switch to enable laser power.
6. Press the  Direct Marking button in Template tab in SmartGraph to make a single pass mark onto the laser card. This will
mark an outline of the Synrad Power meter placement. If the template is the wrong size when marking the Power Meas -
urement Template, adjust the scale to account for the actual focus distance.
If using corrugate, mark again until the placement target is clear.
382
Rev AF

Troubleshooting
7. Turn the key switch to the Open position to disable marking.
8. In Smartgraph, open the Power Check template in the Template tab. Import file to laser if not already loaded to the laser
(see Preparing the Power Measurement Template section).
9. Select Parameters and change Marking Intensity to 100% .
10. Click on the Product Registration icon in the right hand toolbar and verify that the Power Check Product Reg is selected.
11. Select the Configuration tab at and select the Product Registration subtab.
12. Ensure Power Check Product Reg is the file name shown in the left side of screen and that the following values are set:
l Start Trigger : Auto
l Consecutive Trigger : Auto
l Registration of product Movement : No product Movement
13. Turn the key switch to the closed position (three o’clock) to activate the laser unit.
14. Place the power meter in the marked outline such that the target is below the lens. In a forward aimed application, tape
the power meter in place being careful not to tape over the target.
383
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
15. Press and hold the RESET/POWER button on the power meter for at least two seconds.
16. Release the RESET/POWER button on the power meter and immediately click the Direct Marking icon in the Smartgraph
template tab to fire the laser.
17. Let marking run for about  5 seconds  and then stop marking when you hear the power meter emit a series of five beeps.
18. Immediately observe and note the wattage displayed on the power meter within 12 seconds before the reading clears
from the meter display.
19. Compare your results to the expected value in Table 11- 8 on the facing page , considering the following:
l The expected output measured at the focusing lens is 13- 20% lower than output at tube, the actual power loss may
be less than 13- 20%.
l The power loss for a BTU unit may be 3- 4% per BTU. If there are multiple BTUs, multiply this additional expected loss
by the number of BTUs in use.
20. If the power output is correct, move on to Perform the Leakage Test .

33x0 20 + 25 + 30 + n/a
3640 60 + 60 + 60 + n/a
Perform the Leakage Tests
There are two different leakage tests to perform.
l High power – requires the use of a power meter.
l Low power – requires the use of thermo- reactive paper
Disable Leakage Protection
1. Login into the Laser Web Interface and login. You can only perform next steps if you are logged in with service level user -
name and password.
2. Select Laser | Config .
3. Click to toggle Leakage Protection Enabled to OFF .
4. Click Save to save your changes.
5. Reboot the laser.
Perform the High Power Test
1. Ensure the key switch is in the open position.
2. Close the key switch and start printing a template which requires a trigger signal for printing but do not trigger the laser .
3. While system is Waiting for Trigger mode, press and hold the RESET/POWER button on the power meter for at least two
seconds and immediately place the power meter back into the same position it was in for the power test.
4. Wait for the power meter to emit a series of five beeps and then retrieve the power meter.
5. Immediately observe and note the wattage (if any) displayed on the power meter within 12 seconds before the reading
clears from the meter display.
6. Allow the system to idle 1 additional minute waiting for a Trigger before continuing on to another test.
7. Stop marking.
8. Open the key switch to disable marking.
385
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Perform the Low Power Test
1. Ensure the key switch is in the open position.
2. Leave the power meter in position as it was for the power test.
3. Place a piece of thermo- reactive paper on top of the power meter.
4. Close the key switch and start printing a template which requires a trigger signal but do not trigger the laser .
5. For up to 1 minute while the system is in Waiting for Trigger mode, check for burn marks or discoloration on the thermo-
reactive paper.
6. Stop marking.
7. Open the key switch to disable marking.
Leakage Test Results and Next Actions
Test Pass Fail Corrective Action if Failed
High 0 Watts output during test  > 0 Watts output during Test and replace the ACC- 3, SC Chameleon, or
Power test MC Chameleon and LIC board
Low No marks or burning on Discoloration, marks, or Test and replace: the laser tube.
Power thermo- reactive paper burns on paper
Enable Leakage Protection
1. Login into the Laser Web Interface and login. You can only perform next steps if you are logged in with service level user -
name and password.
2. Select Laser | Config .
3. Click to toggle Leakage Protection Enabled to ON .
4. Click Save to save your changes.
5. Reboot the laser.
Test Completion Safety Requirement
When the power measurement is complete, delete the Power Check template, parameter set and product registration.
 Ensure Power Check Template is No Longer Selected
Before leaving the laser, ensure that Power Check template is not the active template in Laser Operation tab!!
Leaving Power Check template selected will cause laser to automatically fire the laser, endangering unaware
users.

Troubleshooting
Wrong LIC Detected Error
The same LIC board is used in the 3140/3340/3640, but signals from the laser tube coming into the LIC differ by model. The sys -
tem looks to the system database of the Chameleon board and also the CP- Light board to verify that the model programmed
into the Chameleon and CP- Light matches the signals from the laser tube. This is done to avoid damage to the laser tube by con -
nection of 60 Watt supply unit (50 Volts sent to tube) to a 10 or 30 Watt laser tube (30 Volts expected at tube).
Symptom
l E00212: Wrong LIC Detected (Fatal Error)
Diagnostics
1. Verify that the model of the supply unit and laser head match. The laser head will have a model number on its cover and
the supply unit will have a model number on a plate along with its serial number.
2. Verify that laser model is set correctly in the laser's Web Interface. See "Connect to the Web Interface" on page 398 for
instructions on connecting to Web Interface.
3. Verify the X9 connector on the CPD3 board and X28 on the Chameleon are seated correctly (i.e. the connector is pushed
down and locked in place completely).
4. Verify laser voltage and ground wires from the laser power supply are connected at the X9 connector on CPD 3. Voltage to
X9 pin 3 and Ground to X9 pin 13.
5. Were galvos/CP- Light board changed recently? Was the appropriate CP- Light board installed?
The CP- Light board has a file with a "0" programmed on a brand new board. Once installed on a laser, a code replaces
the "0" upon initial boot up that permanently writes the model number over the "0". Once overwritten, that CP- Light
board may only be used in a 10 or 30 Watt laser if that initial boot was in a 10 or 30 Watt laser. If a brand new CP- Light
was installed in a 60 Watt, the board can now only be used in a 60 Watt laser. This programming can be reset in the
field, but only via CMark in versions 4.9.2 and later (Service user login required).
6. If the error persists, replace the LIC board.
No Strike Error
The 60 Watt laser tube used in the 3640 laser system has a feature which reports when the plasma created inside the tube is lit.
The system internally tries to light the plasma 5,000 times per second and checks to see if lit 10 times per second. When lit, the
system status will be displayed as Laser Ready. In most cases this status should be achieved in 10- 20 seconds. In cases where the
laser has been shut down for an extended amount of time and stored in cold conditions it may take up to one minute to achieve
Laser Ready status.
If the plasma in tube is not lit within 3 minutes the following error will occur:
l Initial Laser Self- Stabilization Process Was Not Successful. Quality of First Mark May be Insufficient.
(SmartGraph/G55/TCS interfaces)
387
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
l No Strike Error (CLARiTY Laser Controller interface)
Remediation
If the above error occurs, it may be confirmed and you may enter marking mode, but the first few marks on products may be
missing or faded. Products should be monitored during beginning of production run for missing or faded code.
If this error occurs repeatedly and without exposure to extended shutdowns and cold temperatures, it may be necessary to
replace the laser tube.
Red Emissions LED Defective Error
(E00210)
The two red Emissions LEDs (one on the membrane keypad and one on the marking head) are a part of the Safety Circuit of the
laser system. If the system detects that either of these LEDs is not functioning, then laser power will not be enabled. When either
is missing, an E00210 - Red Emissions LED Defective Error is generated and shown in the user interface.
Diagnostics
1. Power the laser system OFF.
2. Disconnect the umbilical connector at the rear of the laser head and inspect the pins on the male side, looking spe -
cifically for bent pins or recessed pins that do not protrude as far out as the others. Focus on pin 31 and pin 32 .
If any pin is bent or pushed in:
2.1. Try to reposition the pin temporarily.
2.2. When next possible, replace the laser head internal harness.
If no pins look out of place:
2.1. Reconnect umbilical.
2.2. Tighten the screws thoroughly.
2.3. Reboot and arm the keyswitch to see if the error persists.
3. Check forward and backward resistance on the membrane keypad pins 2- 10. If open, replace the keypad.
4. Check the resistance between leads on the marking head LED. If open, replace the LED.
5. Check continuity on the shutter cable for HDLED_ EMIS_ RET and MBLED_ EMIS_ RET wires. Note that number of wires
and wire color changes at umbilical connector as in Figure 11- 9 on the facing page .
l If open from HIC to Umbilical Connector replace umbilical.
l If open from Umbilical Connector to CP- Light, replace laser head harness.
388
Rev AF

Troubleshooting
F IGURE 11- 9: Umbilical connector wire detail.
Clicking Noise When Interlock Is Open
The laser system opens and closes two relays on the CPD when any doors connected to the two interlock circuits are open. These
two circuits are meant to be redundant and to be used with a double pole switch. When only one circuit is used and the other cir -
cuit has jumper left in place, one of the relays struggles to stay closed. Over time, this will eventually damage the board.
Symptom: Rhythmic clicking noise can be heard from supply unit while Interlock Open.
Remove Jumpers/Bypasses When Work is Complete
Bypassing any interlocks or the key switch places the user in a dangerous situation that can result in laser radi -
ation exposure, skin damage, and eye damage. Ensure connections you jumper for troubleshooting are
properly configured when work is complete .
Solution
If using a single pole switch, use the wire the switch to X9 as shown in Figure 11- 10 on the next page to avoid long term damage.
389
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
F IGURE 11- 10: Wiring for a single pole switch to XP on the CPD board.
On the X9 connector of CPD:
1. Remove the jumper from 15- 19.
2. Wire the single pole switch between pins 15 and 19 .
3. Remove the default jumper from 17- 21.
4. Place a jumper from 15- 17.
5. Place a jumper from 19- 21.
MIC Not Found Error
Laser systems equipped with the Chameleon MCIP can accept an additional board to support EtherNet/IP or PROFINET with a
PLC for a limited set of functions. The MIC board contains the programming and the ports that must be used for EtherNet/IP for
PROFINET.
When installed, the MIC board must be enabled through the Web Interface. If the Web Interface settings do not match the pres -
ence of the MIC, then errors will occur.
Symptom: The Miscellaneous Interface Controller (MIC) was not found or is not supported (E15100).
Diagnostics
l Verify the MIC board connected to Chameleon if it installed in the system.
l Verify that Web Interface | Other | External Interface is toggled to Disabled if no MIC is installed.
390
Rev AF

Troubleshooting
Wrong MIC Detected Errors
Laser systems equipped with the Chameleon MCIP can accept an additional board to support EtherNet/IP or PROFINET with a
PLC for a limited set of functions. The MIC board contains the programming and the ports that must be used for EtherNet/IP for
PROFINET.
There are two different versions of MIC board. There is one MIC board for EtherNet/IP and one MIC board for PROFINET. The
boards contain the firmware specific to that version and this cannot be changed in the field.
Symptoms
l The Interface communications module couldn’t be detected. EtherNet/IP module not found. (E15101)
l The Interface communications module couldn’t be detected. PROFINET module not found. (E15102)
Diagnostics
1. Verify the MIC board is connected to the Chameleon board.
2. Ensure that the correct MIC is installed (Blue dot for EtherNet/IP or Yellow for PROFINET).
3. In the Web Interface, select Other and verify that Interface is set to the correct MIC option (EtherNet/IP or PROFINET)
for the installed MIC.
391
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
SmartGraph Cannot Connect To Laser
Symptom: When attempting to connect to the laser using SmartGraph software, you receive the error, "Cannot Connect to Laser".
1. Verify that the laptop/PC IP address is set to work with the IP address of the laser system port. The first three octets of
the laptop/PC address must match that of the laser and the last octet must be different than that of the laser. Example:
The laser ACC board IP address is 192.168.1.1 so then the PC IP address must be 192.168.1._ _ (number 0- 255 other
than "1").
If the IP address of the laser is unknown:
1.1. Move DIP switch 1 to the ON position.
1.2. Restart the laser system. The laser IP will now be set to default IP of 192.168.1.1.
1.3. Set your PC to use an IP address compatible with 192.168.1.1.
2. Connect to the Web Interface. If you can connect to the Web Interface, a connection from SmartGraph is possible and
the issue is likely a version conflict between the SmartGraph version installed on your PC and the CMark version installed
on the laser.
See "Connect to the Web Interface" on page 398 for directions on connecting.
3. Verify the version of CMark installed on laser and its required version of SmartGraph needed:
3.1. Connect to the laser Web Interface.
3.2. Select Log | Boot Log .
3.3. Determine the version of SmartGraph required.
3.4. Ensure you have installed the correct version on your PC. See "Check Software Versions - Smartgraph & CMark" on
page 431 for directions on connecting to Web Interface and viewing versions.
4. If you still cannot connect, then the issue is with the connection itself.
4.1. Verify the Ethernet cable is in serviceable condition. You should be using a crossover cable, however most modern
PC/Laptop network adapters can auto switch to crossover mode for you.
4.2. Check for activity at the Ethernet status lights on Ethernet ports on Chameleon board.
4.3. Verify that the PC IP address is set on the correct network connection.
4.4. Boot the laser and connect Ethernet cable.
4.5. On the PC, select Start | Control Panel | Network and Sharing Center | Change Adapter Settings.
4.6. Look for the LAN connection that shows "Unidentified Network".
4.7. Unplug the Ethernet cable to the laser and verify the status changes to "Network Cable Unplugged."
4.8. If you see the status change for a different LAN connection, switch your troubleshooting to that connection.
392
Rev AF

Troubleshooting
Waiting for Trigger Status
Symptom: Laser status displays "Waiting for Trigger" and no mark is made.
Diagnostics
1. Verify the Start button has been pressed.
2. Check that interlocks are closed.
3. Ensure that the correct Product Registration (SmartGraph)/Line Setup (TCS/Clarity) is selected.
4. Verify that the Trigger Enable input X25.9 has 24V applied or is jumpered to X25.12. Voltage is necessary to X25.9 for
trigger signals to be acknowledged.
Web Interface Set to Trigger Enable Voltage Needed
If Trigger Enable Voltage needed is set to Default (3x40) or to True and 24V is not applied to X25.9
then Waiting for Trigger status will appear and no mark will be allowed. This condition applies when
using Product Registration/Line Setup is set to an external source (such as a photo eye, proximity
switch, or trigger from a PLC). Ensure that 24V is applied to X25.9 Trigger Enable input in order to avoid
constant Waiting for Trigger condition.
5. Verify that D6 LED (Trigger Status) goes on and off as you flag product detector. If LED does not flash verify wiring and
exchange product detector.
No Mark on Product
Symptom: Product and Mark Count increase, but no mark is observed on product.
Diagnostics
1. Clean the output lens.
2. Verify working distance is correct for the lens fitted to marking head.
3. Adjust marking head so that it is perpendicular to product.
4. Remove any obstructions in beam path such as conveyor guards or fume extraction nozzles.
5. Verify Parameter settings are correct. Increase Intensity setting.
6. Verify Product Registration (SmartGraph)/Line Setup (TCS/Clarity Laser Controller). Are correct Trigger source and Start
Delay selected?
7. Attempt to mark with no product movement. Change product registration to one with No Product Movement and First
Trigger set to Auto and Consecutive Trigger set to None (SmartGraph) or Internal Once (TCS/Clarity). If laser marks
product with no product movement and internal trigger source then laser is capable of marking and problem is with
393
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Product Registration or Parameters.
Attach Product Registration in SmartGraph
Ensure that after creating a Product Registration in SmartGraph you also attach the Product Regis -
tration to the Template in the Template tab. Changes to a Product Registration only take effect with a
Template if attached through Template tab.
8. Verify D20 LED (Trig Connected) on the Chameleon is lit if using product detect. LED is lit if Product Detector is sensed by
Chameleon. The system will report an error if Trigger Rising/Falling (SmartGraph) or External Rising/External Falling
(TCS/Clarity) is selected in the user interface. Voltage and Ground for the product detector must be attached to X25.4
(Voltage) and X25.8 (ground) or the No Trigger Detector Connected error asserts.
9. Verify D16 LED (Encoder Connected) on the Chameleon is lit (if using encoder). LED is lit if encoder is sensed by the
Chameleon. The system reports an error if Encoder is selected in the user interface and no encoder is sensed. The encoder
supply line must be connected to X25.2 and ground to X25.6 or No Encoder Detected error is asserted.
10. Verify that D6 LED (Trigger Status) goes on and off as you flag the product detector. If the LED does not flash, verify wir -
ing and exchange the product detector.
F IGURE 11- 11: Chameleon board LEDs related to Trigger and Encoder functions.
394
Rev AF

Troubleshooting
Poor Print Quality
Symptom: Mark quality is unacceptable.
First steps in troubleshooting poor print quality
l Clean output lens.
See "Lens Cleaning Procedure" on page 289 .
l Verify and if necessary correct working distance.
See "Working Distance" on page 174 .
l Ensure marking head is as perpendicular as possible to area on product being marked.
Quality Unacceptable, Correct Spacing
Is issue with entire code (faint or scorched)?
l Yes, the whole code is faint or scorched - Adjust Marking Speed or Marking Intensity in Parameters (SmartGraph) or Mark
Settings (TCS, Clarity)
l No, only parts of characters are affected - Proceed to next step
Is the issue with beginning of stroke (use Show Jumps button in mark field toolbar in SmartGraph to show marking order)?
l Yes, the issue is at beginning of stroke - adjust Jump Delay of Jump Speed is waves/squiggles at beginning of stroke or Laser
On- Delay in Parameters (SmartGraph) or Mark Settings (TCS, Clarity Laser Controller).
l Yes, issue at end of stroke - adjust Stroke Delay or Laser Off- Delay in Parameters (SmartGraph) or Mark Settings (TCS, Clar -
ity Laser Controller).
l Yes, curved portions of letters wider than straight portions - adjust Mark Delay in Parameters (SmartGraph) or Mark Settings
(TCS, Clarity Laser Controller).
Is product wet or covered in ice?
l Water droplets and ice crystals refract the laser energy. Use an air knife to dry product area or move laser to different place
on production line where product will not be wet or ice covered.
Has the product changed?
l Changes in the product material or the manufacturing process can affect Parameter settings necessary to mark product or
make product unable to be marked.
Does the product contain calcium carbonate?
l Calcium carbonate reflects the CO2 wavelength and makes marking the material difficult or impossible.
Crooked Code, Characters Overlap, Misshapen Letters
Is Mounting Position/Orientation of marking head correct in software?
395

ection of Product Movement are correct. In TCS/Clarity, verify line direction is correct.

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Connect to the Web Interface
1. Before beginning work, ensure that you know the IP address of the laser system.
2. Set the PC's IP address so that it is on the same subnet as the laser. The first three octets of the IP address are the subnet.
For example: If the laser's IP address is 192.168.1.1 then you set the PC address to 192.168.1.x (x can be any number
from 2 - 254. So your IP address could be set to 192.168.1.5. If the subnet portion of the address (e.g. 192.168.1) is not
the same as the laser, you will not establish a connection with the laser using any of the laser software interfaces.
If the laser's IP address is unknown, Dip Switch 1 can be moved to the ON position. After power cycling the laser, the IP
address for Ethernet ports will be set to 192.168.1.1.
3. Connect a crossover Ethernet cable to the desired PC/laptop and to the Ethernet port on the rear of the supply unit. Altern -
atively, you can connect the laser side directly to one of the two Ethernet ports on the Chameleon board inside the supply
unit. This may be the case if a network cable were plugged into the standard Ethernet port and the optional user interface
port has a pinned connector.
4. Boot the laser (if not already booted). Do not attempt to connect until the laser has finished boot process and the white
light on the Supply Unit keypad has stopped flashing and is on solid.
5. Launch Internet Explorer program on PC. You may also use Firefox.
6. Type into the address field the IP address of the laser system and press Enter.
Specify Port to Connect to Multi– Core Chameleon Web UI
Multi- Core Chameleon boards host the TCS+ interface on the default HTTP port. You must add port
8080 to the address when entering it in your browser to access the Web UI instead of TCS+, e.g.
192.168.1.1:8080 .
The Web Interface Home screen should appear.
F IGURE 12- 1: Web Interface Home Screen.
398

described in separate sections, these include: getting Permissions, editing the Laser Configuration, uploading an AllPrint
Basic file, and updating CMark software.

Troubleshooting Connection Issues
If unable to connect check the following:
l Are the first three octets of PC's IP address the same as laser and last octet different than laser?
l Was the IP on the correct connection changed?
l Was the IP address for the laser typed in correctly in Internet Explorer address bar?
l Try turning off VPN and wireless.
l Add laser's IP address to list of trusted sites in Internet Explorer | Tools | Security.
l Which position is dip switch #1 in? If in On position, connect with default IP address. If in Off position, you must connect
with customer primary IP address.

Edit Laser Configuration
You can view or edit system database information through the Laser Web Interface. Should you replace the Chameleon board,
you must replace the default information in the board's system database with the correct settings for the laser in which you have
installed it.
If you do not update system information, the data mismatch may cause faults and render the laser inoperable.
Editing the Laser Configuration
The following steps must be accomplished to edit the laser configuration saved in the system database.
1. Boot the laser.
2. Connect to the laser Web Interface and Log In.
3. Edit Laser Configuration
4. Reboot System.
5. Reconnect to the Web Interface and verify the settings were maintained.
Edit Laser Configuration

sonnel.

Options & Functions
l Laser Type : Selection must match the actual laser type or errors will assert in the user interface.
l Marking Head Type : Selection must match the marking head type in use or errors will assert in the user interface.
l Lens Type : Incorrect lens type may result in distorted markings and incorrect size of available marking field in user inter -
faces (such as SmartGraph).
l Serial Number : Enter the serial number of the laser here. The serial number is found on a sticker located on the supply unit
rear panel.
l Name : Optional, unless using Videojet Remote Service (VRS). When using VRS, this is where the Friendly Name for the laser
is entered.
l Tube Length : Always set to 0 unless otherwise directed by authorized Videojet engineering or technical support
l IP : IP address of the Ethernet port on Chameleon board. There are two Ethernet ports on Chameleon board, but the
IP address will be the same for both ports unless Secondary IP  is enabled. IP addresses are not tied to upper or lower port.
Subnet mask : Enter the subnet mask for Ethernet ports on Chameleon board. Both ports will have the same subnet mask
unless secondary IP address and subnet mask are entered and enabled.
l Secondary IP enabled : When toggled on, you can enter and use a second IP address.
l Secondary IP : If Secondary IP enabled is toggle on, you can enter second IP address here.
l Secondary Subnet mask : Enter the subnet mask for secondary IP address, likely the same as the other IP address unless
the network administrator says otherwise.
l Gateway : A Gateway is not necessary for direct connections from your laptop, but is necessary when connecting the laser
to a network.
l Timezone : Select the correct timezone from the drop down list.
411
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
l Pilot Laser installed : Tells the system whether a pilot laser is installed in the system. Toggle ON if there is a pilot laser
installed or if you have just finished installing one. Options for using pilot laser in user interfaces may not be displayed if
value is toggled OFF.
l Pilot Laser Zero Position X : Used when calibrating pilot laser to match positioning of CO2 laser beam.
l Pilot Laser Zero Position Y : Used when calibrating pilot laser to match positioning of CO2 laser beam.
l Pilot Laser Scaling Factor X : Used to calibrate marking length on X axis to the output mark of CO2 on X axis.
l Pilot Laser Scaling Factor Y : Used to calibrate marking length on Y axis to the output mark of CO2 on X axis.
l Safety Circuit : Select the type of safety circuit used in the system from the dropdown selector. An incorrect setting here
could cause errors or inoperable laser until selection is corrected.
l Leakage Protection Enabled : Always toggled ON. When ON, the shutter will close after 9 seconds of no product move -
ment. The shutter will reopen again when product starts to move. This feature prevents fires due to beam leakage when the
product is stopped below marking head.
l Trigger enable voltage needed : Set to Default (3x40) - true. Lasers other than the 3340/3140 /3640 using Chameleon
board may not have Trigger Enable input and may be set to false.
TriggerEnable Voltage Settings
If Trigger Enable Voltage needed is set to Default (3x40) or to True and 24V is not applied to X25.9
then Waiting for Trigger status will appear and no mark will be allowed. This condition applies when
using Product Registration/Line Setup is set to an external source (such as a photo eye, proximity
switch, or trigger from a PLC). Ensure that 24V is applied to X25.9 Trigger Enable input in order to avoid
constant Waiting for Trigger condition.
l Disable Trigger/Encoder detached errors : Only available since CMark 3.4. Enable if using trigger or encoder signals, but
not using 24V output from X25 connector to power trigger/encoder.
l System Integration Mode : Not functional on Videojet 3340/3140 /3640 models.
412
Rev AF

Web Interface Tasks
Disable No Trigger/Encoder Attached
Errors
When an external trigger source or encoder are specified in the Product Registration (Line Setup) the system looks for current
drain on specific outputs of the Customer Interface. The system monitors connector X25 pin 2 for Encoder and pin 4 for Trigger
(Product Detect) for a current draw of at least 20 mA. When the load is not present one of the following error messages is dis -
played: "E10813: No Encoder Connected" or "E10814: No Trigger Detector Connected." An example of this situation would be
using a dry contact for trigger or sharing one encoder for multiple lasers. Starting with CMark version 3.4, these errors may be dis -
abled through the laser's Web Interface.
CMark Versions Prior to 3.4
Prior to CMark version 3.4, it is necessary to install a 4.7 KOhm resistor on connector X25 pin 2 to 6 if the applic -
ation is not using the Encoder 24V output, but Encoder is selected in Product Registration/Line Setup. If the
application is not using the Trigger 24V output but Product Registration calls for external source, you must
install a 4.7 KOhm resistor from X25 pins 4- 8. In such applications where CMark cannot be updated to version
3.4, then it is still necessary to use resistor (s). The option to Disable Trigger/Encoder Attached Errors will not
appear in Web Interface on lasers with CMark older than version 3.4.
Disabling Trigger/Encoder Detached Errors
1. Connect to Laser Web Interface by opening Internet Explorer and typing in the IP address of the laser.
2. Log in to the Web Interface.
service
User name
123@service
Password
3. Select Laser | Config .
413
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
4. Click to toggle on Disable Trigger/Encoder Detached Error . A check should appear in the box.
5. Click Save and wait for a prompt to reboot.
6. Reboot the laser. Errors will no longer appear when not using 24V output for the Trigger or Encoder.
Expected Behavior
With errors disabled in the web interface, the following behavior will be exhibited when Encoder or external Trigger are selected in
Product Registration/Line Setup, but not using 24V output. These behaviors will not affect performance of laser:
l Encoder Connected LED (D16) on Chameleon will not illuminate.
l Trigger Connected LED (D20) on Chameleon will not illuminate.
l Encoder ChA Status LED (D18) / Encoder ChaB Status LED (D2) will function normally.
l Trigger Sensor Status LED (D6) on Chameleon will function normally.
l TCS software - Product Detect and/or Encoder green lights in Home Screen and Line Setup will not illuminate.

Web Interface Tasks
Updating CMark via the Web Interface
Using the Web Interface, you can update the CMark operating system stored on the laser system SD card without removing the
card. The CMark Update through Web Interface requires that the SD card already has the necessary bootloader file on it either
through having been loaded from Videojet or by using the XMarkCopyTool to load CMark at least once on the SD card.
Loading CMark through the Web Interface is the only option on laser systems with Chameleon boards with multi- core processor
as there is no SD present. CMark is stored in Flash memory and you can only update CMark through the Web Interface.
Restoring Backups
CMark 4.0 and later allows you to backup and restore your CMark. Backup restoration is the same process as
updating except that you choose a backup file instead of an update file.
To update CMark software using the Web Interface you must:
1. Connect to the Web Interface .
2. Update CMark
3. Restart the laser
Required Items
l Flat blade screwdriver for opening supply unit (if backing up AllPrint Basic or tcl files)
l SD card reader (if backing up AllPrint Basic or tcl files) - single core Chameleon only
l Ethernet crossover cable
l CMark software saved locally (SAR version if applicable)
l (When applicable) A previous CMark backup - multi- core Chameleon only

F IGURE 12- 14: CMark Update shown in the Other drop down menu of Web Interface

Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
8. Click Open . You will be returned to the previous window.
9. Click Upload File to begin the upload process. The selected CMark is loaded into RAM, but upgrade is not started yet.
After the system acknowledges the file, a screen will appear and you can choose whether to maintain the customer's
database. If Inherit Customer Data is toggled on, the Database containing templates, fonts, graphics, etc. will be
backed up and then reloaded onto the card as part of the update process. The system will also maintain any AllPrint
Basic and TCL files loaded on the laser system without any additional steps for backup.
Select Customer Data Source
In CMark version 4.1 and newer, you may choose where to inherit the customer data from including
from an SD card. The system only recognizes the following sources for inheriting data: internal
(EMMC), SD card, and USB. No other locations may be selected. USB and SD card will only be shown as
options in the dropdown menu if the SD or USB is plugged in.
Versions of CMark older than 4.1 do not have this feature.
10. Click the Update button to begin the upgrade process. Click Discard to abort the upgrade process.
The update process may take up to 10 minutes. Be patient.
When the process is completed, the screen will show the message "Done" and the system will go into Standby mode.
BIOS Update
Following some CMark updates, it may sometimes be necessary to update the BIOS on the system.
This is done automatically following some CMark updates. It is very important not to interrupt this
process! ! The user interface will display a message with a timer counting down from 5 minutes and
indicating you should not disconnect power during this update. If you disrupt power during the
BIOS update the Chameleon is no longer usable and will need to be replaced . After the BIOS
update is finished a message will indicate that it is necessary to disconnect power. The keypad power
button (Start/Standby) is inoperable during and after the BIOS update. It is therefore necessary after
the BIOS update to completely power down the system by either turning off main power switch on rear
of supply unit or by unplugging system.
11. Press the Power/Standby button to restart the system.
12. Reconnect to the web interface after the boot process is complete.
13. Select About and verify that the correct version of CMark is loaded. You do not have to log in to view this information.
CMark Backup
You can create a backup of the CMark files, including the Customer Database, through the Web Interface. For those familiar with
Chameleon or ACC board based laser systems, the result of this process would be the same as making a copy of the SD card.
418
Rev AF

Web Interface Tasks
l The backup will include CMark and all Customer folder information such as Templates/Jobs, Parameter/Mark Sets, Product
Registrations/Line Setups,TCL files, and AllPrintBasic files.
l System Database entries are not included in the CMark Backup. System Database includes the laser type, head type, lens
type, IP addresses, serial number, and laser name.
l Only perform a CMark Backup when the system is in good, working order.
l The backup process can take from 8- 10 minutes.
l A shorter backup of only the customer Database can be done more frequently and independently of CMark Backup to keep
an updated backup of the customer database.
Minimum Version Required

laser shutdown.

Enable MIC
Either EtherNet/IP or PROFINET industrial protocols are possible when a MIC - EtherNet/IP or MIC- PROFINET board is installed
on the Chameleon MCIP. The MIC must be enabled and configured before use of EtherNet/IP or PROFINET can be accom -
plished.
Two versions of MIC board
There are two different versions of the MIC board: one for EtherNet/IP and one for PROFINET. Each has its
own part number and is specific to the protocol. It is not possible to change the firmware and software on the
MIC if the wrong part is ordered.
After installation of appropriate MIC board, complete the following steps to enable and configure.
427
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
1. Connect to the Web Interface.
2. Select Other | External Interface from the Home Screen menu.
3. Select either EtherNet/IP or ProfiNET  from the list in the External Interface Settings page.
EtherNET/IP and PROFINET Enable
The following images show EtherNET/IP screens, but there is no difference in the procedure for
PROFINET.
428
Rev AF

Web Interface Tasks
4. Enter into the Host Name field the network name of the PLC (optional).
5. Click to toggle DHCP one or off as needed. DHCP is enabled by default.
6. If DHCP is toggled OFF, enter a static IP address and the address of hte network Gateway for the MIC only. If you do not
yet have the Gateway and subnet information, contact the local network adminstrator.
7. Click Save button.
8. Wait for at least 2 minutes while the system refreshes and connects to the MIC. No reboot is required. When the MIC
has been discovered, the system will refresh the screen and the Status in the window will show as Active. Additional
information such as MAC Address and Firmware Version will also populate.
429
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
9. Plug in the cable from the PLC to either port on the MIC and that port will change from NoLink to Connected if ther is
activity on the connected PLC cable. To test without PLC connected, you may connect Ethernet from either port on
Chameleon board to the MIC and the status of Port1 or 2 should change. Remember to put Ethernet cable back to its ori -
ginal position after test is complete.
The laser system is now configured for use with an appropriate PLC. Customer documents regarding programming such
as GSD, EDS, and Sample Projects, should be provided to the customer to program their PLC for use with the laser sys -
tem.

user interfaces such as TouchUS/TCS.
