TCS+
This user interface can be accessed via any supported web browser connected to the same network as the laser, or using a
TU440/TU430 touchscreen monitor. Many of the features covered here also apply to the legacy TCS software, which was avail -
able on single core Chameleon laser units. You will see it noted here where features not available in the legacy TCS interface are
described.
TU440 Overview
The TU440 is a touchscreen for use only with TCS+ running on multi- core Chameleon boards. There is no processing on board
the TU440, so the unit cannot run the legacy, PC- based versions of TCS software nor any other PC- based software. The TU440 is
a direct replacement for the previous TU430 model, but the TU440 uses all new parts including a new display.
Features of TU440:
l Two USB ports (left side of unit)
l Connection to 3210 via specialized HDMI connection
l IP65 rating
l 4 meter cable length
F IGURE 12- 16: TU440 with the USB ports cover in place on the left side of the touchscreen.
433
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
F IGURE 12- 17: TU430 touchscreen (discontinued).
There are two USB ports located on the TU440 usable for file transfer or connecting a USB mouse. A USB keyboard can be con -
nected to the TU430 on lasers running CMark versions older then version 4.5. CMark version 4.5 and newer will not recognize a
USB keyboard.
The USB cover rotates for access to the ports.
F IGURE 12- 18: USB port cover in the open position.
Laser units connect to the TU430 through a specialized HDMI connector and cable which carry both signal and power for the
touchscreen.
434
Rev AF

TCS+
Possible Equipment Damage - Powered HDMI Port
Never plug a standard monitor into the laser HDMI port as voltage at the HDMI port will damage the monitor.
TU430 is a passive display to interface with the TCS+ program running on the Multi- Core Chameleon board. You can calibrate
the TU440/TU430 touchscreen via the TCS+ software. See "TU440/TU430 Screen Calibration" on page 439 .
There are two versions of the TU440 as there were for the TU430:
l AL- 82418 - TU440 for 3x40 CO2 or 7230/7330/7340/7440 fiber lasers.

l AL- SP78053 for CO2
l AL- 79737 for 7230/7330/7340/7440 fiber laser
l AL- 82785 - TU440 for 7510/7610/7810 fiber and UV lasers.
l Modifications to the cable allow for connection and grounding inside supply unit.
l No external port is necessary.

Cable and Connectors
The cable for the TU430 is replaceable, but note that it does not utilize a quick disconnect at the screen side. The cable connector
has an IP65 housing for use with 3140/3340/3640/7230/7330 laser units. The cable connector fits into a connector on the rear
of 3140/3340/3640 and 7230/7330/7340/7440 supply units.
F IGURE 12- 20: (Left) Cable from TU430 with IP65 housing installed. (Right) Connector for TU440/TU430 on supply unit of
3x40 laser.
The IP65 housing is incompatible with 7510/7610/7810 lasers as the connector fits directly onto a connector on the Chameleon.
The IP65 cover prevents the cable from fully inserting to the connector on the board. The AL- 82785 part number for TU440 is
specific to 7510/76107810 fiber and UV lasers. There is no housing on the connector and the cable is stripped so that applying a
cable tie to hold the stripped portion against the grounding bar inside a 7510/7610/7810 supply unit creates a shield ground.
436
Rev AF

TCS+
F IGURE 12- 21: HDMI connection on 7510/7610/7810.
Hot Plugging the TU440/TU430
So long as no external USB memory drive is plugged into the TU440/TU430, you may hot plug or unplug the unit from the laser
at any time. However, if a USB drive is plugged into the TU440/TU430 hot plugging may cause file corruption on the
USB memory drive. While a USB memory drive is inserted, only plug/unplug the TU440/TU430 connector at the laser when the
system is off or in Standby mode.

be ordered.

TU440/TU430 Screen Calibration
Screen calibration for the TU440/TU430 touchscreen is available through the Main Menu of the TCS+ software.
Only available on TU440/TU430
Screen calibration through the TCS+ software is only available on the TU440/TU430. In legacy deployments
of previous touchscreen models (TU420, TU420H) TCS ran on a Windows based computer. For TU420 and
TU420H, calibration the touchscreen through Windows settings.
1. Press the Menu button at the top of the screen.
439
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
2. Press Main Settings in the dropdown menu.
3. Press Start to the right of Calibrate Screen. This menu option does not appear when using a web browser on a PC/tablet.
The Calibrate Screen option only appears when connected with a TU430.
4. Follow the on- screen prompts to touch the center of the red cross on the screen as it moves throughout the calibration
process. The black "x" represents the on- screen mouse button and can be ignored during this process.
440
Rev AF

TCS+
Best Results with Stylus
For best results use a stylus to touch the red cross during the calibration process.
Possible Equipment Damage
Do not use an open pen or marker. Do not use any metal tool that could scratch the screen.
If calibration process is started from Main Settings, but no interaction on screen is sensed within 60 seconds the cal -
ibration process will abort.

Event Log
TCS+ records an Event Log that can be used to track actions performed by all users. These actions include user login/out, chan -
ging or editing Jobs, Mark Settings, Line Settings, etc. made anywhere in TCS+. Furthermore, laser system reactions, such as errors
and warnings, will also write to the log.
l Actions in other user interfaces such as SmartGraph are not recorded in the TCS+ Event Log.
l Event Log functionality is not supported in PC- based TCS.
View Event Log (CMark 4.9 and newer)
Sufficient Access Rights Required
A user must have sufficient Access Rights to view the Event Log. To change a user's Access Rights, another user
with an Administrator role must edit the Access Rights for the Role assigned to the User.
1. Select Main Menu | Roles and Access Rights .
2. Select the Role to edit.
3. In Configuration , change View Event Log to the Eye icon.
To view the Event Log:

l General
Toggle on Rolling event log to avoid warning or error messages when the log file size limit is reached. When the

you will be notified when the size of the log file is close to the internal limitation. Warnings occur at 5 MB and
error messages assert at 10 MB.
Rolling event log is enabled by default.

before the actual action or action group can be performed.

Adjust Pilot Laser Modes
Only available in CMark 4.9 and up
This functionality is only available on lasers with CMark operating system version 4.9 or newer. Older versions
of CMark do not include this functionality.
On laser models equipped with a pilot laser, different patterns can be drawn with the pilot laser to demonstrate the currently
defined marking region. Not all Videojet lasers have a pilot laser installed. A pilot laser is an optional accessory that is either part
of the beam source itself or a separate piece that is installed into the head. The pilot laser emits a visible red beam that can dis -
play either the message to be marked or a number of different patterns but does not actually mark the product. The pilot laser is
useful for setup in static applications as the image displayed by the red pilot laser strobes while on, but does not wait for encoder
pulses or triggers. Because the pilot laser is not based on encoder pulses or trigger signals, it is not often used in mark on the fly
applications.
Pilot Laser Care
In all Videojet lasers other than the 7340/7440, the pilot laser beam is a 600- 700 nm and is considered Class
2 laser radiation. This laser radiation may cause damage to the eye when staring into the beam.
It is safe for the eye if natural defense mechanisms (blink reflex, turning the head) are not suppressed when
unintentionally looking into the beam.
Use appropriately rated laser safety glasses or beam shield enclosure to prevent eye damage.
456
Rev AF

TCS+
Activating the Pilot Laser
A pilot laser installed in the system must be activated in the system configuration before it is available for use. Activate the Pilot
Laser in Laser Unit Settings | Pilot Laser . Once activated, the pilot laser remains available for use by other users with sufficient
Access Rights as described in "Enabling the Pilot Laser" below .
Enabling the Pilot Laser
1. Press the Main Menu button in the Home screen.
2. Press the Pilot Laser option in the dropdown menu displayed.
3. Press the Mode you would like the pilot laser to mark.
4. Close the Keyswitch if not already closed. The keyswitch must be closed for the pilot laser to function.
5. Select the desired mode.
More than one Mode in the left column may be used at the same time.
Modes in the right column can only be used individually.
The Pilot Laser will be active immediately after the desired Mode button is pressed and will remain active until the Stop
Pilot Laser button is pressed or timeout occurs.
457
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Avoid Prolonged Unnecessary Use of Focus Finder Mode
The method used to provide the Focus Finder feature can tax the galvos if used for prolonged periods
of time. Do not leave the Focus Finder mode on unnecessarily for prolonged periods of time to avoid
accelerated wear and tear on galvos.
6. Press the Stop Pilot Laser button to stop the pilot beam at any time.
7. Press Close button to exit and return to Home screen.

Backup & Restore
You can use the File Management option in TCS to create a backup of the complete laser database, which includes all TCS jobs,
fonts, line setups, and marking sets.
By default, the backup file name is written as the IP address of the laser and the backup date. For example, if the laser IP is
192.168.1.1 and the backup was created on May 27, 2017, the file name will be IP_ 192.168.1.1_ backup_ 05_ 27_ 2017.
Create Backup of Laser Database
1. Touch the Menu button on the Status Bar.
2. Touch File Management . The File Management screen will open.
3. Touch Complete Laser Image on the Laser Local (left side) of File Management window. The number next to Complete
Laser Image refers to the number of files stored in the image.
459
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
4. Touch the Pen next to Complete Laser Image .
5. If using TU440/TU430 : Touch Backup to Device in the context menu that opens.
l TCS will export the entire database to the USB device as one complete file. Files are not written to the USB device indi -
vidually. To import/export only individual files to/from TCS, use the File Import and Export option.
l While transferring, if any of the files already exist on USB, a confirmation dialogue opens with the duplicate file name
and asks if you want to continue. Touch OK to continue the transfer of files or Skip to stop the transfer of a file to
USB.
l When Copy All is toggled ON, TCS copies all files without confirmation.
l You cannot copy an existing font or logo.
l If using PC with web browser: Touch Download . The file will be downloaded to the browser's specified down -
load folder. This is usually the PC's Downloads folder (default).
6. Touch Close to close the File Management screen and return to the Home screen.
7. Restart TCS to see the Backup on the USB device or in the Downloads folder on the PC. Until you restart the program the
backup is not visible on the USB device in TCS.
460
Rev AF

TCS+
Restore a Backup to Laser
1. Touch the Menu button on the Status Bar.
2. Touch File Management . The File Management screen will open.
3. Touch Complete Laser Image on the USB (right side) of File Management window. The number next to Complete Laser
Image refers to the number of files stored in the image.
4. If using TU440/TU430: Touch the Pen next to Complete Laser Image and then touch the Restore button to the
right of Complete Laser Image you wish to restore.
5. Touch OK to confirm when prompted.
461
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
6. Touch OK to continue. A message warns you that continuing will erase all TCS files on laser and not to exit during the res -
toration process.
7. Touch OK to continue.
8. When the file transfer is complete, restart the Touch Control Software.
Firmware (CMark) Update
Using TCS+ version 4.7 or newer, you can update the CMark operating system. Loading CMark on the Chameleon with TCS+ can
only be done when a CMark already exists on the Chameleon. Since TCS+ is part of CMark you cannot use TCS+ to load CMark
on a brand new Chameleon because there CMark and TCS+ don't exist on the board and therefore you cannot connect to TCS+.
If loading CMark to a brand new Chameleon you must use the laser Web Interface to load CMark the first time.
Restoring Backups
TCS+ with CMark 4.7 or newer allows you to backup and restore your CMark via TCS+. Backup restoration is
the same process as updating except that you choose a backup file instead of an update file. For details on
how to create a CMark backup see "Firmware (CMark) Backup" on page 465 .
To update CMark software using TCS+ you must:
1. Update Firmware (CMark)
2. Restart the laser
Required Items
l Ethernet crossover cable
l CMark software saved locally (SAR version if applicable)
l (When applicable) A previous CMark backup - multi- core Chameleon only
462
Rev AF

TCS+
Update Firmware (CMark)
1. Connect to the laser via TCS+ as a user with sufficient Access Rights to update Firmware. By default only the Service user
has Access Rights to this function. Service user can assign this right to other users.
Must Connect via Primary IP with Dip Switch 1 Off
TCS+ functions that upload to or download from the laser require that you connect to the primary
IP address set in Main Menu/Laser Unit Settings/Network menu .
l You cannot upload to or download from the laser when connected via the default IP with Dip Switch 1
in the On position if the primary IP configured is different from that default.
l It is also not possible to upload/download using the secondary IP (if it exists).
l Only the primary IP with Dip Switch 1 in Off position will allow for functions with file transfer in TCS+
or in the laser Web Interface.
2. Select File Management from the Main Menu in the Home screen.
3. Click Upload in the File Management window to select the .zip file containing the target version of CMark. The Open
dialog window will open. It does not matter what type of file is selected in File Management window. Based on the type
of file that you select, the system will put the file in the appropriate location on the laser.
4. Navigate to the directory in which the file is stored.
463
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
5. Click to select the desired file.
6. Click Open . You will be returned to the previous window and a progress bar will show upload progress. This is only for the
upload of the CMark or backup into RAM. There are additional steps needed before the CMark on the system will be
overwritten.
After the system acknowledges the file, the Firmware Update screen will appear and you can choose whether to main -
tain the customer's database.
7. As appropriate, toggle Keep Customer Data and select the Customer Data Source.
l If Keep Customer Data is toggled on, the Database containing templates, fonts, graphics, etc. will be backed up and
then reloaded onto the card as part of the update process.
l The system will also maintain any AllPrint Basic and TCL files loaded on the laser system without any additional steps
for backup.
l By default, the location of the Customer files is set to Internal Storage, but a customer database from an SD card can
also be recognized if the SD was inserted before the update process was begun. The drop- down menu will allow you
to select from Internal Storage or the SD card in that case.
464
Rev AF

TCS+
8. Click the Start button to begin the upgrade process and allow the process to complete. Clicking Cancel will abort the
upgrade process.
The update process may take up to 10 minutes. Be patient.
When process is completed screen will show the message "Done" system will go into Standby mode.
BIOS Update
Following some CMark updates it may sometimes be necessary to update the BIOS on the system. This
is done automatically following some CMark updates. It is very important not to interrupt this pro -
cess! ! The user interface will display a message with a timer counting down from 5 minutes and indic -
ating you should not disconnect power during this update. If you disrupt power during the BIOS
update the Chameleon is no longer usable and will need to be replaced . After the BIOS update is
finished a message will indicate that it is necessary to disconnect power. The keypad power button
(Start/Standby) is inoperable during and after the BIOS update. It is therefore necessary to completely
power down the system by either turning off main power switch on rear of supply unit or by unplug -
ging system.
9. Press the Power/Standby button to restart the system.
10. Reconnect to TCS+ after boot process is complete. Login as any User.
11. Press Videojet logo button in upper right of Home screen.
12. Select the About button in from the drop- down and verify that the correct version of CMark is loaded.
Firmware (CMark) Backup
It is good practice to occasionally create a backup of the CMark stored on the laser system. This backup will include not only the
CMark operating system, but also the customer database (Jobs, Fonts, Logos, etc.) and TCS+ interface with its User information
(configured Users, Roles, Access Rights, Passwords, etc.). This backup can be loaded if file corruption is suspected on the system or
if a Chameleon board needs to be replaced. Also included in the backup are any SAR/TCL files that have been loaded to the sys -
tem, AllPrintBasic files, and Lens Compensation files. These files as well as TCS+ jobs or job elements can also be backed up sep -
arately through the File Management tab if desired.
CMark version 4.7 or newer
Firmware backup function only exists in CMark version 4.7 and newer.
File Creation and Download Time
Creating the backup can take 20- 30 minutes because of the size of the file. It is not advised to interrupt the
backup process.
465
Rev AF

Create a Firmware Backup
1. Connect and log in to TCS+ as User with access rights to create backup. By default, the Administrator and Service level can
create backups, though these Users can delegate rights to other Users in Roles/Access Rights.
2. If using a TU430 touchscreen, insert a USB drive with at least 500 MB of free space into the TU430 USB port. If using a
web browser on PC, then it is not necessary to use USB. It is not recommended to create backup using TU430 port as the
speed of the ports is extremely slow, and length of time required for backup creation could be confused for failure.
3. Press the Main Menu button.
4. Select File Management from the drop- down menu.
5. Scroll down to Firmware option in the window.
6. Press the Pen button.
7. Press the Download button.
466
Rev AF

TCS+
Download Will Take 20- 30 Minutes
Be patient as the creation and download of the Firmware backup will take considerable time. Before pressing
Download button be aware that process will take 20- 30 minutes. There is no progress bar.
Immediately after pressing the Download button, the system will create a backup of CMark firmware and when complete will
download to either USB drive in folder called TCS if using TU430 touch screen or into Downloads folder on PC when using TCS+
via web browser (depending on web browser settings).
When the download is complete the TCS+ screen will return to File Management menu. You can now use PC to browse to Down -
loads folder and move file and rename if desired. Do not change the file extension of the backup. It must be a .zip file. Do not
open the backup and then rezip using Windows. The backup can be corrupted by Windows.
For instructions on how to load the backup to laser, see "Firmware (CMark) Update" on page 462 .
TCL Script
TCL scripts are files used to add specific additional functionality to the laser system. SAR files are delivered as TCL Scripts.
The following rules apply for TCL scripts to be uploaded:
l The TCL script to be uploaded to the laser must be in a .zip file format and cannot be any other file type.
l TCL scripts must also have the startup.sh located at the root level within the .zip archive. If the startup.sh file is within a
folder inside the .zip archive then the TCL script will not run.
l Only one TCL script can be uploaded. Loading a second TCL script will overwrite the previous one.
l If two TCL scripts need to be loaded to the laser, the SAR group must combine them into one package.
Upload
1. Connect and log in to TCS+ as a User with access rights to upload a TCL script.
2. If using a TU4x0 touchscreen, insert into the TU4x0 USB port a USB drive containing the desired TCL script in the proper
format. The file must be in a folder called TCS on the USB drive if using a TU4x0. If using a web browser on a PC, then it is
not necessary to use a USB memory drive.
3. Press the Main Menu button.
4. Select File Management from the dropdown menu.
467
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
5. Press the Upload button at the top of the File Management window. It is not necessary to select TCL scripts from the list
first. When you select the file from the Windows Explorer window, the software will put the file into the correct file location
based on the file type you upload.
TCL Scripts must be formatted correctly for upload
Formatting Requirements
l The file must have .zip file extension. It can have any file name, but needs to be .zip file.
l There must be a startup.sh file at the root level of the .zip.
l Only one TCL Script can be loaded. If multiple TCL scripts are required they must be combined by the
SAR group into one .zip. Loading a second TCL Script will overwrite the first TCL Script.
6. Select the TCL script for to be uploaded from Windows Explorer if using a web browser or from the USB drive if using a
TU4x0. The upload should not take more than a few seconds.
7. Press the OK button to acknowledge the dialogue box that notes a reboot is required.
8. Reboot the laser system to finish installation of TCL Script. Script is active after reboot.

that can be uploaded to the laser system through the Web Interface.

between the .zip being loaded and the startup.sh file within that .zip.

Laser Unit Settings
In CMark 4.7 and newer, the Laser Unit Settings in TCS+ take the place of the former laser Web Interface. Laser Unit Settings are
accessed in TCS+ via the Main Menu. System settings available in this menu are dependent on the Access Rights granted the cur -
rently logged in User. These Access Rights can be edited by other users with sufficient privileges.
The only action that must be done through the laser Web Interface and not through TCS+ Laser Unit Settings is uploading
CMark to a Chameleon which does not already have CMark installed. Since TCS+ is part of CMark, you cannot access TCS+ until
472
Rev AF

TCS+
CMark is loaded. The laser Web Interface would be required for uploading CMark when a new Chameleon is installed or after the
Rescue Stick procedure is performed. After CMark is loaded, you can update CMark through TCS+ from that point forward.
The Laser Unit Settings in TCS+ (CMark 4.7 or newer) contains the following functions (view depends on User):
l Laser Configuration l Arc- Compensation
l Date and Time l Lens Compensation
l Pilot Laser l Remote Service
l Network l System Database
l Permissions l Logging
l External Interfaces l Multi Laser Configuration (CMark 4.9+)
l Allprint Basic l Trigger Queue (Cmark 4.9+)
Edit Laser Configuration
When troubleshooting, or if a Chameleon board has been exchanged, it may be necessary to set the Laser Configuration. If a
new Chameleon board has been installed it is necessary to install CMark via the Laser Web Interface before the Edit Laser Con -
figuration page is available.
You cannot load CMark via TCS+ on a new Chameleon because TCS+ doesn't exist until CMark is installed.
1. Login as Service User in TCS+ using the daily password. Admin also has access to some of the functions in Laser Con -
figuration, but not all functions.
2. Select Laser Unit Settings from the Main menu in Home Screen.

473
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
4. Edit functions or information as necessary based on the definitions detailed in "Options & Functions" below .
5. Press Save . Reboot is required after any changes are made and Save is pressed. A Fatal Error is shown until laser is shut
down and rebooted.
Options & Functions
l Load Barcode Scan
For manufacturing only. Loads laser configuration from work order.
l Reset CP Light
When a CP- Light is installed in a laser system a code is written to the CP- Light upon first boot that indicates the model,
wattage, umbilical length, and marking unit type. Once this code is written to the CP- Light the board can only be used in
that laser configuration. If CP- Light and marking unit are to be tested or used in a laser system with different umbilical
length or wattage then the CP- Light code must be reset or errors will occur. This button can only be accessed by Service
user.
l Serial Number
Enter the serial number of the laser here. The serial number is found on a sticker located on the supply unit rear panel.
Only the Service user can change/set the laser type.
l Name
Optional, unless using Videojet Remote Service (VRS). When using VRS, this is where the Friendly Name for the laser is
entered.
474
Rev AF

TCS+
l Marking Head Type
Selection must match the marking head type in use or errors will assert in the user interface. Only the Service user can
change/set the laser type.
l Tuning
Routine used for galvo control. Selections are either Quality (default) or Speed. High print speed text applications may be
better with Speed selected.
l Lens
This value should be set to the currently installed lens.
l Tube Length
Always set to 0 unless otherwise directed by authorized Videojet engineering or technical support. By default, only Service
can change this setting, but it is possible for the Service user to hand over Access Right to this setting to Admin or other
user.
l Working Distance
Set by manufacturing, this is the optimal distance for the specific laser system and marking head installed as measured
from marking head output to the product. This setting cannot be adjusted in the TCS+ software by any User and the best
working distance may vary based on the material being marked and the effect desired. The Working Distance is saved in
the CP- Light during pairing of marking unit and CP- Light. Never separate a CP- Light and marking unit set.
l Safety Circuit
Select the type of safety circuit used in the system from the dropdown selector. An incorrect setting here could cause
errors or inoperable laser until selection is corrected. Only Service user can change/set the laser type.
l Leakage Protection Enabled
Always toggled ON. When ON, the shutter will close after 9 seconds of no product movement. The shutter will reopen
again when product starts to move. This feature prevents fires due to beam leakage when the product is stopped below
marking head.
l Trigger enable voltage needed
This may be set to True if the application dictates that both Trigger and Trigger enable must be present to print.
l Disable Trigger/Encoder detached errors
Only available since CMark 3.4. Enable this if using trigger or encoder signals, but not using 24V output from X25 con -
nector to power trigger/encoder.
l System Integration Mode
Changes the behavior of the System Ready output. Not recommended to change on most Videojet laser models. Leave
this set to Stand Alone Laser . By default only Service can change this setting, but it is possible for the Service user to
hand over Access Right to this setting to Admin or other user.
l Simmer
This control is no longer supported, though it may be displayed. Changes cannot be made by the user.
Controls first pulse behavior (7340/7440 only).
475
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training

Network Settings
Chameleon- based lasers have two Ethernet ports that can be configured with unique IP addresses through the Laser Unit Set -
tings within the TCS+ user interface. The two Ethernet ports on the Chameleon act as a switch and the IPs assigned are not spe -
cific to the upper or lower Ethernet port.
Configuring IP Address (es)
Configured IP Address (es) only take effect when Dip Switch 1 on Chameleon board is set to Off and the laser is rebooted after
the change. If the IP address of the laser is unknown, setting Dip Switch 1 on the Chameleon board to ON and rebooting will use
a known IP address to connect. After connection, you may connect and edit the IP address to a desired, unique IP or may dis -
connect or view the configured IP and then use the configured IP in the future. Make sure to set Dip Switch 1 back to the Off pos -
ition and reboot after either discovering the configured IP or after setting a new unique IP address.
If using the TU430 touchscreen to connect, no IP address is needed because the TU430 connects via specialized HDMI.
1. Login as Service User or as a User who has been granted Access Rights to Network Settings within Laser Unit Settings..
2. Press the Main Menu button and select Laser Unit Settings from the dropdown menu.
477
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
3. Press the Network Settings option in the left column of the window displayed. It may be necessary to scroll down to
this option.
4. Enter the Primary IP address desired and the Subnet mask.
5. Enter a Secondary IP address and Subnet mask if desired.
6. Enter a Gateway if needed for the network.
7. Press the Save button.
8. Press the Close button.
9. Shut down the laser and, if necessary, move Dip Switch 1 to the Off position.
10. Reboot the laser for the changes to take effect. IP address changes take effect on boot up completion.
Permissions
Any computer on the same network and subnet as the laser can communicate with the laser system, however you must register a
PC's IP address to the laser to allow fire commands from that PC to the laser when using the SmartGraph user interface. IP
addresses can be added to the list with Permissions through TCS+ on lasers with CMark 4.7 or newer.
Adding an IP address to the laser's Permission may be necessary when:
l Servicing a printer - register your laptop
l Installing a laser system - register the PC or touchscreen computer that will control laser
478
Rev AF

TCS+

1. Login to TCS+.
2. At the Home screen, select Laser Unit Settings from the Main menu.
3. Select Permissions in the left column on the Laser Unit Settings screen. The IP addresses that have Permissions on the
laser system will be shown in the right column.
4. Click Add to enter an additional IP address. The system will display a dialogue box with the IP Address of the device you
are connected with. Edit the IP Address if that is not the desired IP Address for which to add permissions.
479
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
5. Press OK . The list of IP addresses with permissions will display and the one just entered should appear in the list.

External Interfaces
When an MIC - EtherNet/IP or MIC- PROFINET board is installed on the Chameleon MCIP, the EtherNet/IP or PROFINET industrial
protocols are available, respectively. You must enable the MIC and configure it before the application can use EtherNet/IP or
PROFINET.
Two Versions of MIC Board - Ordering Guidance
There are two different versions of the MIC board: one for EtherNet/IP and one for PROFINET. Each has its
own part number and is specific to the protocol. It is not possible to change the firmware and software on the
MIC if the wrong part is ordered.
After installation of the appropriate MIC board, complete the following steps to enable and configure it in TCS+.
CMark 4.7 or Newer Required
An MIC board can only be enabled and configured through TCS+ on a laser with CMark version 4.7 or newer.
CMark 4.5 is the minimum version level required to support the MIC. If CMark 4.5.x is installed on the laser,
you must instead enable the MIC in the laser Web Interface on the External Interface page.
Enable and Configure a MIC Board
1. Connect to TCS+ and log in as a User with sufficient Access Rights to enable the External Interfaces.
2. Press the Main Menu button.
480
Rev AF

TCS+
3. Select Laser Unit Settings from the dropdown menu.
4. Press the External Interfaces option in the left pane.
5. Press the button next to Interface Type in the right pane and select the desired type of MIC board / Communication,
EtherNet/IP or PROFINET .
481
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
6. Set the IP configuration. Press the box next to DHCP to toggle on Dynamic Host Configuration Protocol.
- OR-
Configure settings manually to use a static IP address. The procedure for setting IP is the same for both PROFINET or
EtherNet/IP.
6.1. Type the desired address into the IP Address field.
6.2. Type the network Subnet Mask into the Subnet Mask field.
6.3. If necessary, enter the network Gateway IP address into the Gateway field.
F IGURE 12- 22: In example shown, DHCP is not used and the MIC IP address is set to 192.168.1.5 and MIC board of type
EtherNet/IP is selected.
7. Press the Save button.

TCS+

enabled and ready to use.
By default, Enable Webserver and Firmware Update are only available when logged in as Service user. Thes may be
made available to other users, but only if the Service user changes the Access Rights to allow that user to view/edit these
items.
9. Connect the cable from the PLC network to the MIC.
10. Reboot the laser with the cable connected to MIC. Errors occur when industrial network cable is connected or dis -
connected to MIC while already booted. You can confirm and clear this Error in any User Interface.
The laser system is now configured for use with appropriate PLC. Customer documents regarding programming, such as GSD,
EDS, and Sample Projects, should be provided to the customer to program their PLC for use with the laser system.

Communications Monitor
When an MIC board is installed in the laser system and enabled through the External Interface menu in either the Web Interface
or TCS+ (CMark 4.7 or higher), the Communications Monitor is available. The Communications Monitor displays inbound and out -
put information between the laser and the PLC. Only inbound information accepted by the laser is shown. This functionality is
only available when connected to a PLC and either EtherNet/IP or PROFINET are installed and set up in the laser system.
Some, but not all, commands from the PLC to the laser are reflected in the TCS+ or SmartGraph user interfaces. The Com -
munications Monitor functionality in TCS+ allows the user to see all commands accepted by the laser, even those not shown in
the user interface.
Accessing the Communications Monitor tool
1. Connect to TCS+ and log in as a user with Access Rights to the Communications Monitor tool. Usually, Service and Admin
roles have access by default.

Arc Compensation
When printing on the top or bottom of objects that are rotating around a central point, you can use Arc Compensation to com -
pensate for the non- linear motion in order to mark in the desired spot.
Arc Compensation is enabled and settings entered through the Web Interface or TCS+. The following provides a brief overview of
the setup through TCS+.
F IGURE 12- 23: Examples of code with Arc Compensation not enabled (left) and enabled (right).
An example of an application that would use this feature is when the laser will mark on the tops or bottoms of vials/bottles that
are rotating on a star wheel (as shown in Figure 12- 24 on the next page ). Each vial or bottle sits in one of the openings and
passes under the laser marking head as the wheel rotates. The product therefore travels under the marking head in a non- linear
path. Arc Compensation mitigates the effect of this type of movement so that the marking appears straight as if the product
had been moving in a linear path.
491
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
F IGURE 12- 24: Star wheel like those used in marking pharmaceutical bottles and vials.
Arc Compensation Settings
X- Offset: X Value in millimeters between rotation center of the roundtable and the center of the marking field (distance marked
green).
492
Rev AF

TCS+
F IGURE 12- 25: White line shows X Offset.
Y- Offset: Y Value in millimeters between rotation center of the roundtable and the center of the marking field (distance marked
blue).
F IGURE 12- 26: Example of X and Y offset where settings would not equal 0 .
Ratio (Table: Encoder): The encoder to table ratio. Example: if the encoder is mounted in the center the ratio equals 1. DiamEn -
coder and DiamTurnTable can be used to configure a transformation ratio between Encoder and Turntable.
493
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Pulses per Encoder Turn:
The rotation direction can be changed by the sign of the PulsesPerEncoderTurn value. Positive values will configure counter clock -
wise rotations. Negative values will configure clockwise rotations. This setting is used instead of Rotation which is later in the
same screen.
If printed square appears as follows try a negative value.
Start Angle: Angle between line from marking field center to rotation center and line from Product detector to rotation center.
Positive values mean angles against movement direction.
494
Rev AF

TCS+
Lock Angle: Angle between two products in degrees (decimal point is allowed). Within this angle all incoming detector pulses are
blocked. (Similar to blocking).
495
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
F IGURE 12- 27: Example of Lock Angle.
Start- Lock Angle: Only important for special trigger mode StartTrigger: rising, ConsecutiveTrigger: Auto, StopTrigger: Falling. In
this scenario, the StartLockAngle is the angle within which a start trigger is blocked after stoptrigger went in (in degrees/decimal
point is allowed).
Start Trigger: Select from four options in dropdown. This defines the type of signal that will be used to start the first mark in the
process.
Consecutive Trigger: Select from dropdown menu to define which type of trigger will be used after the first trigger to mark all
products that come after the first.
Stop Trigger: Select from the drop down menu to define what type of trigger, if any, will be used to stop the marking process. If
set to None, the laser will continue to mark products until user presses Stop in interface.
Rotation: Indicates the direction the table is turning. Options are clockwise, counter clockwise, or None. This should always be set
to None. Pulses per Encoder Turn should be used (negative or positive number) to indicate clockwise or counter clockwise dir -
ection for turntable.
Examples of Installations
The following illustrations are examples of laser and turntable possible installs.
496
Rev AF

TCS+
F IGURE 12- 28: Example of laser centered on both X and Y axis of turntable.
If the laser is mounted on the bottom of a clockwise turn table then the X offset is the measurement between the center of the
turn table and the center of the marking field. If the center of the laser marking field is centered with the turn table the Y offset is
0. Because the encoder is mounted to the shaft in the center of the turn table the Ratio (Table: Encoder) is 1. If using a photocell,
the angle between the X axis and the angle of the photocell must be entered as the Start Angle. Rotation should be set to None.
497
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
F IGURE 12- 29: Example of laser not centered on X axis of turntable.
If the laser is mounted on the bottom of a clockwise turn table, the X offset is the measurement between the center of the turn
table and the center of the marking field. The Y offset is the measurement right or left of center of turntable. Use negative values
if the laser is to the left of center, positive values if the laser is mounted to the right of center. If using a photocell, the angle
between the X axis and the angle of the photocell must be entered as the start angle.
498
Rev AF

TCS+
F IGURE 12- 30: Example of laser on the left side of a turntable rotating in a clockwise direction.
In a clockwise direction with the laser to the left of the turn table, the Y offset would be the distance between the center of the
turn table and the center of the marking field. Because the laser is mounted on the left side of the turn table, the Y offset will be
a negative number. The X offset would be the up or down measurement along the X axis from center. If the Y offset is centered
with the marking field, X offset would be 0.
499
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
F IGURE 12- 31: Example of laser on the left side of a turntable with X axis off center and clockwise rotation.
In a clockwise direction, if the X is off center and down, the X offset compensating value will be positive. If up the X offset will be
a positive value.
F IGURE 12- 32: Example of laser on the right side of a counter clockwise turn table centered.
500
Rev AF

TCS+
In a clockwise direction with the laser to the right of the turn table, the Y offset would be the distance between the center of the
turn table and the center of the marking field. The Y offset will be a positive number. The X offset would be the up or down meas -
urement along the X axis from center.
Setting Up Arc Compensation
Before You Start
Before beginning this procedure, either ensure that the PC connected to the laser has an IP address defined to
communicate with the laser system. You can alternatively use the TU430 touchscreen monitor. In TCS+, you
must be logged in as a User with sufficient Access Rights to create jobs and to edit Arc Compensation.
1. After the laser is mounted to the turntable, create a 20 mm by 20 mm square in the center of the marking field as
shown below. If available, you can also use the Kompensation logo instead.
2. Print the message to determine the center of the marking field in static mode (no product movement).
501
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
3. Press the Main Menu button in the Home screen and select Laser Unit Settings .
4. Stop the laser. Always stop the laser in TCS+ before making changes to the Arc Compensation settings.
5. Press the Arc Compensation option in the left hand column of Laser Unit Settings screen.
502
Rev AF

TCS+
6. Enable the Arc Compensation by pressing the box to the right of Enable Configuration in the right hand side of the
screen. A check mark should appear in that box.
7. Enter settings that describe the turntable in the Arc Compensation page and press Save when complete.
Arc_ config.xml Is Not Found Message
A warning may display indicating that the Arc_ config.xml is not found. This is normal if no Arc Com -
pensation was previously configured. This error will clear after Arc Compensation settings are entered
and Save button is pressed.

Remote Service - Enable VRS &
Remote Access
CMark 4.7 or Newer
The following descriptions only apply to lasers with CMark 4.7 or newer. Older versions of CMark do not con -
tain the ability to enable Videojet Remote Service (VRS) through TCS+, nor do they have the ability to
remotely access TCS+ or CLARiTY interface on laser.
Videojet Remote Service (VRS) can be enabled through either the laser Web Interface or through TCS+. When set correctly, the
laser may then be remotely accessed and TCS+ or CLARiTY interfaces may be seen by the remote user. The SmartGraph user
interface cannot be accessed remotely, though the laser itself can be seen remotely for the general dashboard.
The following instructions show how to enable VRS on the laser through TCS+. For information on how to access the laser
remotely see either "Locally Enable Remote Access via TCS+" on page 511 or "Locally Enable Remote Access via CLARiTY Laser
Controller" on page 512 .
Enable VRS on Laser
1. Connect to TCS+ using TU430 or via a web browser by typing IP of laser into address bar of browser.
2. Login as Admin or Service.
3. Select Laser Unit Settings from the Main menu in upper right corner of the Home page.
504
Rev AF

TCS+
4. Select Network from the left column.
5. Enter the desired Primary IP (typically IP of device for user interface/HMI).
6. Toggle on Secondary IP Enabled and enter a Secondary IP , which will be the one used for VRS connection.
7. Press the Save button.
8. Shut down the laser.
9. Change the IP address of your laptop or PC  (if necessary) to connect on the new Primary IP of the laser.
10. Reboot the laser.
11. Connect to the laser using the new Primary IP address of the laser.
12. Login to TCS+ as either Admin or Service.
13. Select Laser Unit Settings from Main menu in upper right corner of Home page.
14. Press Remote Service in left column of the page.
505
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
15. Toggle on Enabled Remote Service .
16. Enter the IP address of the Remote Edge Server (RES) in the Remote Edge Server IP field.
17. Type in the IP address of the CLARiTY Laser Controller connected to the laser in the Remote Access IP if using a CLARiTY
Controller. If using TCS+ or SmartGraph as the interface, you can leave this box blank or you may type in the secondary
IP address of the laser.
18. Checkmark for Remote Access Available is temporary and will time out. The customer must enable the remote access
session here before a remote user can take control of the laser. The session will time out after 20 minutes of inactivity at
the RES
506
Rev AF

TCS+
19. Press the Save button and shut down the laser system. On reboot, Remote Service on the laser is activated, but does
require that the RES be active as well.
Remote Access Session VRS
If Videojet Remote Service is enabled on a laser with CMark 4.7 or newer, a user with sufficient access (including Videojet Service
and Technical Support) can take control of the laser if the local user grants permission. The remote user can only use the Remote
Access Session with TCS+ or CLARiTY interfaces. SmartGraph is not available remotely.
To start a remote session, the local user must first enable this session on the laser through the TCS+ interface. If the local user
has a CLARiTY interface, they must first connect via a PC to the laser and also enable the session through TCS+. There is no way
to directly enable a remote session in the CLARiTY Laser Controller.
See the following for instructions on enabling a remote session on the laser (local):
l "Locally Enable Remote Access via TCS+" on page 511
l "Locally Enable Remote Access via CLARiTY Laser Controller" on page 512
The following section describes how to start a remote session on the remote side.
Remote Access (User has TCS+)
1. Log on to the VRS Connect site.
2. Find the specific printer you want to connect to via serial number search or by browsing to the customer account/site.
3. Access the printer's details page.
4. Click the Remotely Access Printer button.
5. Connection will be established to the laser and the browser will display TCS+ no matter what the customer's user inter -
face.
507
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
6. For Videojet Service/Tech Support: Log in to TCS+ as Service user. This is important because you will then be logged out
of customer's laser after 20 minutes of inactivity at the RES. It is also important so that you are logged as Service user in
the Event Log on the laser.
Remote Access (User Has CLC)
1. Log on to the VRS Connect site.
2. Find the specific printer you want to connect to via serial number search or by browsing to the customer account/site.
3. Access the printer's details page.
4. Click the Remotely Access Printer button.
5. Connection will be established to the laser and the browser will display TCS+ no matter what the customer's user inter -
face.
508
Rev AF

TCS+
6. For Videojet Service/Tech Support: Log in to TCS+ as Service user. This is important because you will then be logged out
of customer's laser after 20 minutes of inactivity at the RES. It is also important so that you are logged as Service user in
the Event Log on the laser.
7. To continue to CLARiTY interface see instructions below.
8. Click on the Main Menu button in upper right of Home screen.
9. Select Laser Unit Settings from the dropdown menu.
10. Click on the Remote Service option in the left hand column of Laser Unit Settings window.
11. Click on the Remote Access View button at the top of right hand portion of the window.

Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Ensure that the Remote Access IP in the same window is set to the CLARiTY laser controller's IP. If it is not then you
need to change to the CLARiTY laser controller's IP and save. Customer will then have to reboot laser and then CLARiTY
laser controller and then enable remote session again on a PC before you will be able to connect.
CLARiTY display should then be shown in your browser.
12. Click the Close button when done with remote session.

Locally Enable Remote Access via TCS+
A remote user can only take control of the lasers with VRS and CMark 4.7 or newer if the local user first enables the remote ses -
sion on the laser. The following procedure shows the steps necessary on the local user end to enable a remote user to take control
of the laser via Videojet Remote Service .
Personal Injury - Laser Can Be Fired Remotely
The remote user will have the ability to fire the laser. Care should be taken that people and property are not in
the line of fire. All beam shielding should be in place and interlocks closed.
Enable Remote Session on Local Laser
1. Login as a user with sufficient Access Rights to enable a remote session. By default, only the Service and Administrator
users can enable a remote session in TCS+ software, but both of these users can grant Access Rights for Remote Service to
other Roles.
2. Press the Main Menu button in Home screen.
3. Select Laser Unit Settings in the dropdown menu.
4. Press the Remote Service option in the left pane of the Laser Unit Settings page.
5. Press to toggle on Remote Access Available .
511
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
The Remote Access session is now enabled and a warning box will display to the user to ensure that it is safe to fire the
laser.
6. Press OK button.
Personal Injury - Laser Can Be Fired Remotely
The remote user will have the ability to fire the laser. Care should be taken that people and property
are not in the line of fire. All beam shielding should be in place and interlocks closed.
7. Press the Save button.
8. Press the Close button to view Home screen.
Locally Enable Remote Access via CLARiTY Laser Controller
If using a CLARiTY Laser Controller as the user interface on a Videojet Remote Service connected laser, it is possible to enable a
remote session, but the local user must enable the session using TCS+ and not directly in the CLARiTY Laser Controller. Once the
remote session is enabled in TCS+, the remote user will be able to reach the CLARiTY Laser Controller. It is not possible to directly
enable the session through the CLARiTY Laser Controller directly.
The following section describes how to connect through TCS+ to enable a remote session when the normal user interface would
be CLARiTY Laser Controller.
512
Rev AF

TCS+

l PC or laptop
l Ethernet cable
l Must know IP of laser
Personal Injury - Laser Can Be Fired Remotely
The remote user will have the ability to fire the laser. Care should be taken that people and property are not in
the line of fire. All beam shielding should be in place and interlocks closed.
Connect to TCS+ to Enable Remote Access for CLARiTY Interface
1. Connect a PC or laptop using Ethernet cable to one of the two smaller Ethernet ports on the CLARiTY Laser Controller. Do
not use the port with the large circle around it.
2. Set the IP address of PC/laptop to onecompatible with IP address of laser. For example if the laser IP Address is
192.168.10.221, then you would want to set the PC/laptop to 192.168.10.xx where xx is something between 1 and 255, but
not the same as the last digits of the laser's IP.
3. Open any web browser.
4. Type in the IP address of the laser. A page will display with a list of users.
5. Click on the user named Service . Ask Videojet Technical Support or Service for the daily password required to enter this
user level.
6. Press the Main Menu button in Home screen.
7. Select Laser Unit Settings in the dropdown menu.
8. Press the Remote Service option in the left pane of the Laser Unit Settings page.
513
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
9. Press to toggle Remote Access Available .
10. Ensure that the Remote Access IP is set to the IP address of the CLARiTY Laser Controller.
Remote access session is now enabled and a warning box will appear warning the user to ensure that it is safe to fire the
laser.
11. Press the OK button.
Personal Injury - Laser Can Be Fired Remotely
The remote user will have the ability to fire the laser. Care should be taken that people and property
are not in the line of fire. All beam shielding should be in place and interlocks closed.
12. Press the Save button.
13. Press Close button to view Home screen.
14. Press the Log Out menu button in upper right corner of Home page.

TCS+

System Database
The System Database stores the configuration of the laser system in an writable EEPROM on the Chameleon board. This con -
figuration includes the model, head type, IP addresses, and system level settings. These items are set in Laser Unit Settings in
TCS+. This System Database is not part of CMark which is saved to the Chameleon separately.
A backup of the System Database can be created and uploaded onto the system in case of replacement of the Chameleon
board. This saves time and prevents error when configuring the new Chameleon board.
The following instructions describe how to create a backup of the System Database and how to load the backup, if necessary.
Create Backup of System Database
1. Connect to TCS+ and login as a User with sufficient Access Rights to access Laser Unit Settings. The Service User can both
Create and Upload System Database. Administrator by default can only Create the Backup. The Service User can change
Access rights for any Role to change this default.
2. If using TU4x0 touchscreen, insert the USB drive into TU4x0 before creating backup.
3. Press the Main Menu button.
515
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
4. Select Laser Unit Settings from the dropdown menu.
5. Press System Database in the left pane.
6. Press Download button next to Download current system database from laser .
If using a TU4x0, the backup of System Database will be saved to the USB drive inserted in the TU4x0. If using a web
browser, file will be sent to your Downloads folder. The file is automatically named [Laser Type] [Laser Name] [Date cre -
ated].zip. You can rename the file, but do not change the file extension of the backup.
516
Rev AF

TCS+
Load a Backup of the System Database
1. Connect to TCS+ and login as a User with sufficient Access Rights to access Laser Unit Settings. The Service User can both
Create and Upload System Database. Administrator by default cannot load a System Database to the laser. The Service
User can change Access rights for any Role to change this default.
2. If using TU4x0 touchscreen, insert a USB drive into TU4x0 before starting the upload.
3. Press the Main Menu button.
4. Select Laser Unit Settings from the dropdown menu.
5. Press System Database in the left hand column of display.
6. Press the Upload button next to Upload system database to the laser .
517
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
7. Browse to the System Database file to be loaded to laser and select.
8. Reboot the laser when prompted.

Core Dump
On rare occasions, Videojet Technical Support may request a Core Dump if the operating system on the laser has crashed. In the
event of an operating system crash, the system automatically generates a report. This report is only useful to the software
developers and should only be provided if requested. The following instructions detail how to download the report from TCS+ in
CMark version 4.7 or newer.
519
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
Do Not Perform This Procedure Unless Requested by Technical Sup -
port
This procedure will cause the laser to go into a state that requires a hard power down. Using the Core Dump
option should never be performed unless requested.
Download Core Dump
1. Connect to TCS+ and login as a User with sufficient Access Rights to access Laser Unit Settings.
2. Press the Main Menu button.
3. Select Laser Unit Settings from the dropdown menu.
4. Press Logging in the left pane.
5. Press the Create button to the right of Core Dump.
6. Press the Yes button when dialogue box asks if you want to create Core Dump. The system will go into an error state and
white and yellow lights will flash.
7. Power down the system using the main power switch on rear of supply unit.
8. Reboot the system.
9. Connect to TCS+ and log in.
10. Press the Main Menu button and select Laser Unit Settings from the dropdown menu.
11. Press the Download button. If using a TU4x0 a USB drive must be inserted. The file will be named CoreDump.tar and
will be sent to Downloads folder if using a web browser or the USB drive if using TU4x0.
520
Rev AF

TCS+
12. Press Close .
Trigger Queue
Only available in CMark 4.9 and up
This functionality is only available on lasers with CMark operating system version 4.9 or newer. Older versions
of CMark do not include this functionality.
In early versions of CMark, buffered triggers would be lost when production was interrupted by safety doors being opened. Begin -
ning with CMark version 4.9, the behavior of the trigger queue can be set by the user (as long as that user has appropriate access
rights). Typically this behavior will be configured as part of the installation process.
Only for use with On the Fly applications
Trigger Queue should only be used in applications using an encoder and product detect and marking on mov -
ing products. Best fit is where several products fit between the product detect and laser head. This feature is
not reliable in indexing applications where the product moves to the marking field and stops to be marked.
Set Trigger Queue Behavior
Triggers are only kept in queue when a connected door is opened. Triggers are not retained when an error occurs and in this case
the trigger queue will be emptied.
521
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
1. Login as Service User or as a User who has been granted Access Rights to the Trigger Queue write access.
2. Press the Main Menu button and select Laser Unit Settings from the dropdown menu.
3. Press the Trigger Queue option in the left column of the window displayed. It may be necessary to scroll down to this
option.
4. Press the box next to Shutterlock Open or Interlock Open based on the behavior you would like as described below.
Shutterlock Open : Toggle on if you want to preserve detected trigger signals in trigger queue in case of a shutterlock
open signal. If toggled off, detected trigger signals are removed.
Interlock Open : Toggle on if you want to preserve detected trigger signals in trigger queue in case of an interlock open
signal. If toggled off, detected trigger signals are removed.
5. Press the Save button.
6. Press the Close button to return to the Laser Unit Settings screen.
522

534
Rev AF

Glossary
A
ACC (Advanced Controller Card)
The primary system board of many Videojet lasers. Functionaly equivelant to a motherboard in a PC. The ACC3 is superceded by the
Chameleon board.
aperture
An opening, window, or lens through which optical radiation can pass. The aperture limits the energy or power for measurement of
exposure. (ANSI 136.1 - 2014)
B
beam divergence
Divergence is the increase in the diamater of the laser beam with distance from the beam waist, based on the full angle at the point
where the irradiance (or radiant exposure for pulsed lasers) is 1/e times the maximum value. (ANSI 136.1 - 2014)
beam expander
An optical device that expands the size of a collimated beam. There are generally two types, prismatic and telescopic. Videojet beam
expansion technology utlizes the latter telescopic (or Galilean) beam expansion method.
beam waist
The minimum diameter, in centimeters, of a focused, axis- symetric beam. For non- symetric beams, there may be a beam waist along
each major axis, each located at a different distance from the source. (ANSI 136.1 - 2014)
BTU (Beam Turning Unit)
Beam turning units are precisely machined, modular units containing mirrors inside internal elbow joints. BTUs may be inserted
between the laser head and marking head to redirect marking output to a location otherwise inaccesible to the full laser head and
marking head assembly.
BUS
Beam Unit Straight is a linear tube that allows the marking head to be located away from the laser head thus allowing the marking
head to fit into areas that would be inaccessible to full laser head and marking head assembly. Can be used in conjunction with BTUs.
535
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
C
Controller
One of four main components of a laser system, the controller is the user interface device by which an operator directly interacts with
the laser system.
CPD
Cobra Power Distribution board is the primary safety controller in Videojet lasers that use Chameleon boards. The CPD is the main inter -
face for connections for interlock and shutterlock safety systems. The CPD is always present, but if a higher performance level is neces -
sary then a FASS board may be added to the CPD to bring to Performance Level D.
D
direct marking
From Smartgraph while editing a template, sending a template directly to a connected laser and firing the laser, marking that template
immediately.
DSP
Digital Signal Processor. Controls Laser source control, Marking trigger process, Marking head control, Marking process.
E
ellipticity
A measurable ratio of the electromagnetic fields within a beam, relevant in the context of using devices to measure beam size or
power.
expansion factor
The value by which. through the application of a beam expander, the diamater of a laser beam diameter is increased.
F
FASS
FASS - FOBA Alltec Sicherheitssteuerung (Safety Controller)
focal length
The distance from the secondary nodal point of a lens to the secondary focal point. For a thin lens imagin a distant source, the focal
length is the distance between the lens and the focal point. (ANSI 136.1 - 2014)
536
Rev AF

Glossary
G
gain medium
The source of optical gain in the laser, e.g. the contents of the beam source/laser tube.
I
ID Matrix
Another term for a Data Matrix code.
index step
Index steps allow you to use different marking parameters for various objects within the same template.
irradiance
Radiant power incident per unit area upon a surface, expressed in watts- per- centimeter- squared (W*cm^2). (ANSI 136.1 - 2014)
J
jump
A jump is movement of the scanner mirrors from the end of one stroke to the beginning of the next. The laser beam is OFF during a
jump.
jump delay
Jump Delay is a period of time prior to each jump that the galvos are not moved and the laser beam is OFF.
jump speed
Jump Speed is the speed at which the galvos move during a jump.
L
laser
A device that produces a nearly parallel, nearly monochromatic, and coherent beam of light by exciting atoms to a higher energy level
and causing them to radiate their energy in phase. Originally an acronym standing for (L)ightwave (A)mplification by (S)timulated
(E)mission of (R)adiation.
laser head
One of four main components in a laser sytem, the laser head contains the laser tube and the Fast Move (FM) board.
537
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
laser off delay
At the end of each stroke, delay turning off the laser after the galvo mirrors stop moving.
laser on delay
At the start of each stroke, delay turning on the laser after starting to turn the galvo mirrors for marking movement.
Laser Tube
The hermetically sealed tube contaiining primary and secondary gases through which photons pass and are amplified through stim -
ulated radiation emission.
line width
The Line Width parameter allows you to tell the laser exactly how wide one line marks on the substrate in mm. Greatly affected to the
Overlap parameter.
LVDS
Low Voltage Differential Signal is the means of galvo signal commands from the ACC board to the FM Board or in lasers with
Chameleon boards to the CP- Light board.
M
mark delay
At the the end of each vector, delay the turning the galvo mirrors before marking the next vector.
marking field
The two dimensional plane across which the laser system can mark. At its center is the origin of the X,Y axis (0,0). The X and Y values on
this axis correspond to the X and Y galvos and the positive/negative signal values they receive to mark at a given location within the
plane.
marking head
One of four main components in a laser system, the marking head contains the X and Y galvos that actively and dynamically direct the
beam at the substrate during marking.
marking intensity
Marking intensity is the strength of the beam. In Smartgraph you can adjust marking intensity as a percentage of the maximum power
of the laser marking system.
marking speed
Marking speed is the speed at which galvos move when the laser beam is on.
538
Rev AF

Glossary
Matrix
A matrix splits a single template into multiple discrete cells, each of which can contain unique image and text content or a repeated
pattern of the same image and text content.
MMC
An MMC or Multimedia Card is a flash memory storage device on which the laser operating system is stored. MMCs are a common
flash storage product but have been superseded by SD Cards in both general use and in Videojet lasers.
mode quality
Mode Quality (or M^2) is a measurement of beam quality expressed a ration between the measured beam and a perfect diffraction-
limited Gaussian beam which would have an M^2 = 1.
O
off delay
At the end of each stroke, delay turning off the laser after the galvo mirrors stop moving.
on delay
At the start of each stroke, delay turning on the laser after starting to turn the galvo mirrors for marking movement.
overlap
The Overlap parameter determines how closely to the last line each new line is marked. Overlap is set as a percentage of total line
width from -50% to 50% with 0% representing no overlap at all.
P
Pilot Laser
A small, visiible laser that demonstrates where the marking head will mark. The pilot laser does not mark on substrate.
polarization
In most cases, laser light is linearly polarized. This means that the electric field oscillates in a particular spatial direction.
pulse frequency
Lasers mark not with a continuous beam but rather with rapid laser pulses (CO2, YAG, Fibers). The pulses are marked close together
and overlap so that each stroke appears to be a solid line. The Pulse Frequency parameter allows your control over how frequent the
pulses are, i.e. how much they will overlap.
539
Rev AF

3140 3340 3640 Technician Training
R
rise time
The time required for a pulse to rise from 10 percent to 90 percent of its steady value.
S
SD Card
Secure Digital (SD) is a non- volatile memory card format developed by the SD Card Association (SDA) for use in portable devices. The
standard was introduced in August 1999 by joint efforts between SanDisk, Panasonic (Matsushita Electric) and Toshiba as an improve -
ment over MultiMediaCards (MMC), [1] and has become the industry standard. SD Cards supersede MMCs on ACCs revision L or later.
SLEB (Steered Laser Extension Board)
Adds dedicated Ethernet port, Serial connection (RS- 232), and External Job Selection functionality.
spot size
Diameter of the beam as measured at the optimal focal distance lens in use. Spot size is the diameter of a beam at X inensity at a
given range. In this context the X can be defined as [1/e2 x I_ max] which resolves to a diameter across which the beam reliably
provides 13.5% of the beams maximum intesity. Alteranatively, X may be defined as the half intensity point, or in other words as Full-
width Half- height (FWHH) - the diameter of the beam at which intensity is equal to 50% of its maximum, center intensity.
stroke
A single, continguous mark made by the laser.
stroke delay
At the end of each stroke, delay turning the galvo mirrors before marking the next stroke.
supply unit
One of four main components in a laser system, the supply unit is a case that houses the processing, memory, and power supply com -
ponents.
T
template
A single file that containst the images and text that will be marked by a laser system, created in a design program such as Smartgraph.
540
Rev AF

Glossary
U
Umbilical
The complete assembly of wires and cables that connect a supply unit to the laser head.
V
vector
A vector is one of many small, consecutive line segments that make up a stroke or curve. The number of vectors in a curve is determ -
ined by the design of the font in use and the choices made by the fontographer that created that font.
W
wavelength
The distance between one peak or crest of a wave and the next peak or crest. It is equal to the speed of the wave divided by its fre -
quency, and to the speed of a wave times its period.
